## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — Chapters 0381 to 0390, days 381 to 390

**Chapter 0381 — Nine Steps, And A Page In A Coat Nobody Asks About.** Day 381, a Thursday, the sixty-sixth. The room over the market, about nine people, and **Adrian Vale at the far end of the bench reading a page that is not his, with a sheet folded into four in the inside of his coat with a date on the back and two names on the front, and the coat buttoned over it.** He performs nothing and is asked nothing. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register says out loud, in front of about nine people, that she is not going to ask him what is in the inside of his coat, that she has known there was a page in it since the first day of that month, and that the not-asking has cost her the same as asking would have** — because a page in a coat is the only page in four hundred miles that cannot be asked for, and if she asks, it stops being that. Nobody in the room says thank you for it and she has not asked for that either. **Then the man of about thirty-four turns round on the bench for the first time in about a fortnight and tells the man of about thirty-nine, at the wall by the step at the back, that on the day they come up those steps he is to go before him; the man of about thirty-nine says he is not going to be the first one up them; and the man of about thirty-four says he knows that, and is asking anyway, and worked it out on his own and was never told.** The man of about thirty-four gave the reason out loud before he was asked for it, on condition nobody said anything about it afterwards, and the room agreed without a word. The woman of about twenty-one is at the bench with the pen in her apron and was asked one question in this flood once, on a Friday six days ago, and nobody here is going to be the second one. **Adrian Vale is in this chapter and in no other of the ten, and about two hundred rooms is at zero in it and may not be used in it.** Change: a room spent a whole morning on two things and neither of them was on a form, and what it decided was who walks up a stair first.

**Chapter 0382 — Three Barrows And A Handcart, In Rain.** Day 382, a Friday, the sixty-seventh. The boards at the foot of a market in sideways rain. **Three barrows go up the boards and two of them have belonged to nobody since before the water; the man of about thirty-eight offers them and will not say whose they are, and the reason he gives out loud is that a wheel that has been dry since before the water is a wheel nobody has had to use, and a barrow nobody has used is the only thing in four hundred miles a person can take without anybody being able to say they took it off somebody.** The man of about thirty-four puts his right hand on the first shaft without saying why and nobody asks him why. **The second barrow will not go up wet, and the reason is not the weight: a barrow on a board goes up on its wheel if the board is dry and on its shaft if it is not, and the shaft takes the load into whoever is holding it; the man of about thirty-four holds it about as long as it takes to fill a pail, puts it down in the water, and stands there with his right arm across his chest, and the man of about thirty-eight goes up the second one himself and will not let anybody help him.** The man of about sixty with the handcart comes up behind them with a hazel mallet whose head is bound in cord and a coil of rope he has not used, takes the mallet out, and does not hit anything. The man of about thirty-nine stands at the bottom end of the boards for about an hour and a half in the rain with his board of nineteen rates under his arm and does not touch a barrow and nobody asks him what for. **By the fifth hour there are three barrows under a sacking at the top of the boards, one of them his and two of them nobody's, and one of them for each of the three people who are going, and nobody in this city has written that down.** The man of about thirty-four got his hand open about a quarter of an hour before the light went, by putting it in the cold water, and did not say that he had been unable to close it, and the man of about thirty-eight knew and did not say that he knew. Change: three barrows are at the top of some boards for the first time in this flood, and one of the three men lost the use of his right hand for the afternoon getting one of them up a wet hill.

**Chapter 0383 — One Question In The Open, And A Number That Goes On.** Day 383, a Saturday, the sixty-eighth. The boards of a salt wharf, the only flat thing for about four streets, and a stack of salt at the top end that cannot be got off it by anybody. **The man of about thirty-nine, who has been in about nine rooms in a fortnight without being asked one question in any of them, comes down a hundred steps of river path at the ninth hour and asks one question in the open, where anybody on the path could have heard the answer: *What do you put down.*** The man of about thirty-eight does not stop working and says **about four hundred and forty**, in his own mouth, on the boards, having said it at a stall about nine times in a month to nobody in particular. **He then says, before he is asked, that that one goes on, and that there is another number, and that he is not going to say which of the two he believes and has never told anybody, and that the other one has never been on a page in this city and is not going to be on one this morning, and he says all of it out loud on a wharf and not in a room because a thing said in a room stays in the room and a thing said on a wharf goes up the river.** The man of about thirty-four stood at the bottom end for half of it with his right hand open and shut in his coat pocket, about nine feet off a man with two numbers, and neither of them knows what the other's is for. **The second figure is printed nowhere in this chapter and is not given as a number anywhere in this volume.** The man of about thirty-nine took his board off the boards at the fourth hour with a number and a sentence he did not ask for. Change: a number that has been said at a stall for a month was said for the first time by the man it belongs to, in the open, and a second number was announced and refused.

**Chapter 0384 — The Four Places Read Out Loud, And A Share That Is A Household's.** Day 384, a Sunday, the sixty-ninth. The room over the market, about nine people, and **the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register reads all four places under the heading out loud in the local language, in daylight, with the day on the door, and she has read the other three in about nine different rooms and has not read this one before, because the fourth one is going out on somebody else's calendar.** A district, which has nowhere to start. A population, a number, and the man who sends it is the man who works boards at the bottom of this city and is not in the room and has not been asked what the number is. A rota, read and then a silence. **A share.** She says out loud that a share is a figure, and asks for it to be said out loud in the room that a share is not a figure. The man of about thirty-four, who said twelve days ago in this room that he has never once gone to find out what a share is, says it: **a share is a figure and a share is a household's, and about nine people in this room have been reading the fourth place as a number for a month because a number is what the other three of them are.** Nobody goes to the household. The reason is given in one breath before anybody asks for it: **she said no on a step before anybody had asked her, there is no version of this flood in which anybody asks her again, and so the fourth place stays empty and goes out empty and goes out correct.** The cost of that is said out loud too: the reason the fourth place is empty is a woman on a step and not a column, and no office nine hundred miles off is ever going to be able to tell the difference. **The man of about thirty-nine then says, from the back, the first whole sentence he has said in nine rooms that is not a question: there are two empty thirds in this room, the third place on the form and the third column of the book, and they are not the same empty and he is not going to sit here and be asked whether they are.** The woman who keeps the register does not argue and does not look at him, and not one of the room is going to be the second one to agree with him. Change: about nine people in this city now know what the fourth place is, and the knowing has not put one mark on the page.

**Chapter 0385 — Thirty-Nine Of Forty, Under A Gate, And A Line Still Ruled.** Day 385, a Monday, the seventieth. **Thirty-nine of forty men under a gate at the end of a road out of this city, every working day since the first of March, which was the eighty-fifth day of this flood, and on this morning they are exactly three hundred days unpaid, and the man of about thirty-nine says all three numbers out loud on the road where every one of the thirty-nine can hear him, and the sentence names the eighty-fifth and the three hundred and eighty-fifth, and says that the three hundred is counted from the eighty-fifth.** Nobody argued with him about the number, because the number was right. **The first thing said back to him is that it is the first time anybody has said that number out loud where the man could stand and hear it, and he had nothing to say to that and did not try.** The man of about thirty-four is one of the thirty-nine; he is at the gate from the second hour to the seventh, which costs him a day of selling every working day, and he has never once said so to the person he sells to. A board by that gate has a ruled line at the bottom of a column, ruled with three strokes, gone over never, empty since the first of March. **There is a man under that gate who could have filled it, everybody in this flood knows it, nobody has ever asked him about it, and that is the man of about thirty-four, who writes a line on a board for a living and writes it last and in a harder piece of chalk, and who has a piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket all morning with his hand flat over it.** He does not take it out. At the sixth hour he tells the man of about thirty-nine what is going to happen to him in a room, and gives him the answer in advance, and it is not about the line and not about the days. **The number the man of about thirty-nine said out loud on that road is the first figure anybody in four hundred miles has ever attached to those thirty-nine men, and it is on nobody's page and there is no place in this city for it, and a figure said out loud in the open is the only kind of figure that cannot be got back off anybody.** Change: a cost was said out loud in the open, once, and could not be put back on anybody, and the line is still empty when the light goes off it.

**Chapter 0386 — Offered On A Step, And A No That Was Somebody Else's Idea.** Day 386, a Tuesday, the seventy-first. The bottom step of nine steps, two steps below the boards. **Tamsin Quill takes the folded sheet of road out of her apron, does not put it on her knees, and opens it on the step beside her, on the boards, in the open — the third time in this flood she has opened it in front of anybody and the first time in front of one person.** The woman of about twenty-one comes along the boards, stops about nine feet off, and then comes the rest of the way, because she is not the man who stands nine feet off. She says, standing, before the fourth crease is open: **I am not going to take it.** Tamsin Quill goes on opening it and says the part she wants on the record first: **you did not say no to a thing I put in front of you, you said no to a thing I had already decided, and I opened it on this step in the open because a thing said on a step is a thing said in the open, and not because I wanted you to have it.** The woman of about twenty-one answers that. Then: **I have walked nine places in three days and nobody has asked me what is in them, and that is not what is on that sheet, and you knew it when you put it down.** Tamsin Quill says she knew it, has never sent for anybody's nine places, and did it anyway, and that the woman she did it to is the one who has to carry the having done it. The man of about thirty-four is taking his trestles down nine feet off and no barrow, and has counted the places on that sheet from that distance and said so and asked nothing. **The sheet goes back into the front of the apron folded in four with the same four creases, and the fourth one is the one that gives, and it went into the apron anyway.** Change: a page in this city was offered in the open and refused, and the woman who offered it said out loud that the refusal was of her own decision and not of her offer, and the crease that goes soft is the only part of that page anybody in four hundred miles is losing.

**Chapter 0387 — A Wall With A Shape Going Off It, And Two Men In Front Of It.** Day 387, a Wednesday, the seventy-second. A lane of about nine houses past two gates, ending at a shut gate, with nine feet of bare stone down one side of it and a shape on it that has been going off since a Tuesday, and that is the whole of what it is and nobody in four hundred miles is going to be able to say what it was. **The man of about thirty-four comes along the lane at the seventh hour and stops in front of the wall and does not go up to the market; he has chosen a wall over a board of boards.** The man of about twenty-five comes along at the eighth hour with his coat buttoned, nine words he wrote himself in the inside of it, not said in four rooms, and stops about nine feet off and looks at the shape for a while. He puts his flat hand on the wall at about shoulder height, on the part that has gone softest, says nothing while his hand is there, and takes it off. **Neither of them asks the other one anything, and a lane has no day on a door and no form in four hundred miles with a place for a man standing in front of a wall.** Then the man of about thirty-four says the thing out loud, on the lane, to the wall, before anybody asks him for it: **it is going to be a wall with nothing on it by the end of this week, and I have a piece of chalk in this pocket that I have not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, and I am not going to write on it**, and when the other man says *you have not got anything to say to it*, **the reason I am not going to write on it is that I have not got anything that is mine to put there, and that is the whole of the reason, and it is not modesty and it is not the weather.** The man of about twenty-five says nothing and does not take his words out of his coat. **The woman of about fifty at the shut gate writes a date on the post and does not rub anything out, and says four words out loud to the post, and the four of them are a date, and they are not for either of the two men, and she has never once said a date to a person.** Change: two men stood in front of a wall and neither of them wrote anything on it, and the man with the chalk said out loud in the open that he has nothing of his own to put there.

**Chapter 0388 — A Sheet Ruled Into Two And Not Carried Down The Stair.** Day 388, a Thursday, the seventy-third. The room over the market, and **the woman who writes the notices rules the seventh sheet by hand with the back of a pen, and the first six went out on days 351, 354, 359, 362, 366 and 371, each four days before its room, the six rooms on six different days, and the six sheets not the same number of days apart.** The seventh is for the seventy-eighth day of that month, a Tuesday, and it goes out in the morning and not today, and she says out loud that a sheet made on one morning and sent on the next is a sheet that has been sat on by about nine people. **The man of about thirty-four refuses to change it and gives the reason before anybody asks for it: a thing which changes is a thing somebody can be sent to ask about the difference between, and there is no version of a notice in this city in which anybody four hundred miles off is going to send anybody to ask about a difference between two sheets that say the same thing.** He then says that he has been asked nothing about it in case anybody in the room was about to and has now decided not to, and that he would rather it be in the room than be a thing he was asked for in a corridor. She writes the day, writes the place out long, and leaves the top of it empty for the seventh time, and the reason has been said out loud in that room on six separate mornings. **Then he says the thing that is actually wrong with it: we cannot tell anybody what it is for, we said so out loud in this room a fortnight ago and nobody argued, and it means that on the seventy-eighth day about nine people will come up nine steps and not one of them will know why, and not one of them can be told, and there is no form in four hundred miles for telling them afterwards that they came for the right thing.** The room has no answer. The sheet goes on the table at the fourth hour and does not go down the nine steps. Change: a room made its seventh notice and did not send it, and a notice which is not sent is a room admitting out loud that it does not know who is coming to it.

**Chapter 0389 — The Seventh And Last Sheet Goes Out, To A Place And Not A House.** Day 389, a Friday, the seventy-fourth. The room over the market, with the day written in chalk at the sixth hour, earlier than usual, **because a sheet that goes out early is a sheet that is against a wall before the light is on it.** The sentence is said in the local language, standing, with the sheet in her hand: **this morning is the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month, a Friday, and the room it is for is on the seventy-eighth day of that month, a Tuesday, and that is four days counted from this morning including this morning.** There is a day on the sheet and a place on the sheet and nothing at the top of it, and it is not signed and nobody asked her to sign it. **She says out loud that that is the seventh, that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood, that she has known that for about a week and has not said it before today, and that a person who is going to write one more ought to say out loud that it is the last one while there is still somebody in the room to hear it.** The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living takes it without looking at the face of it, and has carried six and not opened one, and will not say the reason again in front of nine people because he has said it twice. The room laughs and stops. **A man who has carried seven things to four walls without opening one of them is the only person in this city who can be shown to have been in seven places and cannot be shown to have been anywhere.** He puts it against the wall at the top end of the wharf boards with a stone off a doorstep. **The man of about thirty-nine, who works them with a tally-board and a knife, tells the carrier out loud on the wharf that he is not going to be sent for the room, because being sent for is being sent for, and after that there is no way for anybody at the far end of anything to find out whether he went or not**, and the man of about thirty-eight said that number on Saturday and has said nothing else since. Change: the last sheet of paper in this flood is against a wall with a stone on it, and the man who was going to be standing at the bottom of the nine steps has said out loud that he is not going to be one of the people who comes up them.

**Chapter 0390 — What The Three Of Them Are Carrying, And A Saturday With Nothing On It.** Day 390, a Saturday, the seventy-fifth. The room over the market, about nine people by the ninth, and **nothing at all happens in it for the whole morning, and nobody in it decided that.** The register-keeper has not been up the nine steps. The not-asking has been the arrangement in that room since a Thursday sixteen days ago, when a man with a piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket said out loud that they were not going to say what the room was for, and nobody argued, and it has held for sixteen days. **She asks one question and it is two words and a verb — *What will you carry* — and it is not the question she was asked on a Friday six days ago and it is not about whether it will go, and nobody is ever going to ask her what she is for.** The answer is **What I am standing in**, given without taking the pen out of the apron, and it has nothing in it except a pair of boots and a coat. **Then she says the other half of it, to the table: nobody is going to write down what the three of us are carrying, not on a form and not in a book and not on a slate, and that is the only way any of us is going to be able to say afterwards that we were not sent.** The room agrees without a word of argument, and **there is a woman at that bench who could have watched a piece of chalk go onto a slate in that time and did not, and nobody asked her to, and the woman who would have had to ask him for it was not going to.** The man of about forty-eight sat for five hours and shut and opened a folding knife about nine times and said one word, and nobody in the room found that remarkable and about four of them found the opposite. Tamsin Quill keeps the sheet in her apron all morning: **it is not going in anything they take, and it is not going against a wall, and it is not going into a book, and it is not going to be written out on the back of a form, and there is a crease in it that has gone soft, and that crease is the only part of this page anybody in four hundred miles is losing.** The three of them come down the nine steps at about the second hour of the afternoon and nobody counted anything. **A blank is the only thing in four hundred miles a return cannot be filled in from, and the room has known that for a month and has only just said it in front of anybody.** Change: the last decision anybody in this city is going to make about those three before the road was to leave a blank where the column would go.

**The ten chapter files existed on disk when this phase was resumed, and this run's work on them was a repair pass. The entries above describe the files as they now stand, and the first edition of the ten differed in ten arithmetic and reference faults, five bolded turns over the working ceiling of about ninety words and four over the hard ceiling of ninety-five, thirty-six repetitions of a narration frame that restated the sentence immediately before it, four instances of the *about four seconds* device where a chapter allows at most one and two of them in chapters whose finding is a refusal, and five runs of four consecutive speech paragraphs, four of which this run's own repairs had created.** **No day, no weekday, no month day, no notice, no form, no object count, no anchor, no cast member and no decision of record moved, and the first edition and the published edition of every figure are side by side in `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §9.** The volume's own name is spoken once, in Chapter 0357, and is in none of these ten and is not in this note.

**THE NEXT PHASE AFTER THIS BATCH IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and it is a BATCH and it is the LAST batch of Volume 08, and it is not the close.** It writes Chapters 0391 to 0400, days 391 to 400, from a Sunday to a Tuesday, and it carries the resolution, the new question and the last image: day 391 is when three people go down the road with a barrow each, day 393 is the climax and the volume's one panel, day 395 is the register line with a day on it and nothing under the day, day 396 is the new question asked and not answered, and day 400 is the fence and the road going away under it, and four of the volume's seven round-figure traps fall on day 400. **The Volume 08 close is not this batch's and is not the next batch's.**

## NOTE ON CHAPTERS 0381 to 0390 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and it changed no entry above this line

**The ten chapter files existed on disk when the phase that wrote them was resumed from a checkpoint, that phase's work on them was a repair pass, and then a second pass ran on the same ten files after a review found six faults in them. The entries above describe the files as they now stand, and both passes are recorded, because a chapter summary that describes the third edition of ten chapters without saying that there were two earlier ones is the same failure this repository names.** The second pass is at `reviews/volume-08/batch-0004.md` and its pages are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §11 and its figures at that file's §9.11 and §9.12.

**WHAT CHANGED IN EACH OF THE TEN, AND IN SIX OF THEM NOTHING AT ALL CHANGED.**

1. **Chapter 0381 — two sentences.** The man of about thirty-nine was in two places at once: the lead of the second section had him against the wall by the step at the back where the light does not reach, and the fifth section had him in the band of light at the near end of the bench. **He is against the wall at the end of that bench nearest the step, in the light, where the fifth section's observation of the two men is true.** And the gloss on the woman of about twenty-nine's refusal gave the man of about thirty-four *the back of seven rooms in a fortnight*, which is the man of about thirty-nine's count and reaches eight in this same chapter sixteen paragraphs later: **it is gone, and the thirty-four has his own two weeks at the near end of that bench, with no figure on him.** Nothing else in the chapter moved: the woman of about twenty-nine still says out loud that she is not going to ask what is in the inside of his coat and that the not asking has cost her the same as asking would have; the man of about thirty-four still asks the man of about thirty-nine to go up the nine steps before him and still says the reason before he is asked for it; the woman of about twenty-one is still asked nothing; the man of about twenty-five still says nothing; the second slate is still on the shelf with a date on it and nothing under it; Adrian Vale still reads a page that is not his and is asked nothing and performs no working.
2. **Chapter 0382 — five sentences, two of them new narration, no speech touched.** The man of about thirty-nine came along the boards at the ninth hour and went up them at the sixth, in a day whose own hours run two, three, four, five, six and then a light at the seventh that goes again before the eighth: **he comes along at about the third hour now, which is the hour the first barrow goes up and the hour he stands at the bottom end for while the second one will not, and no other hour in the chapter moved.** The clause under *had worked out since that* was in a present tense and then announced the exchange in the next section, and it is past now and the announcement is cut, and the same paragraph's mallet sentence reads *and has not used it* where it read *and is not going to use it*. **And the chapter's governing exchange — four lines that named nobody in a scene holding five men — now has two narration beats that name the man of about thirty-eight as the one who came to ask and the man of about thirty-four as the only other person at that end of the boards, and neither beat sits between a plain speech and the bold that answers it, and the house bold-and-plain pattern is byte-identical.** The three barrows, the two that belong to nobody, the handcart and the hazel mallet, the right hand that could not close, the man of about thirty-nine standing at the bottom end of a wet wharf and the three barrows under the sacking by the fifth hour are all as they were.
3. **Chapter 0383 — no change to any event, one hour.** The man of about thirty-nine came along the boards at the ninth hour and took his board off them at the fourth, which is before he came: **he goes up the path at about the second hour of the afternoon now, in the form this volume already uses, because the clock runs the morning and the afternoon as two passes and an hour after the ninth is not a seventh.** The man of about thirty-eight still said *About four hundred and forty* out loud on the boards in his own mouth for the first time, still announced a second number and still did not say which of the two he believes, still put his knife down flat for the first time in nine years, and still said it all on a wharf and not in a room because a thing said in a room stays in the room. The man of about thirty-four still stood at the bottom end with his right hand open and shut in his coat pocket and said nothing at all. **The clock in this volume runs the morning and the afternoon as two passes, and this chapter's lead puts the light at the sixth hour, so the departure is an afternoon hour and is now written as one.**
4. **Chapter 0384 — no change.** A district, a population, a rota and a share read out loud in the local language in one room with the day on the door, the fourth place going out empty and going out correct, and the woman of about fifty-two's refusal standing on a step and not reopened. The register-keeper is in this chapter and in 0381 and 0388 and is not in the same file as Tamsin Quill anywhere in the ten.
5. **Chapter 0385 — no change.** Thirty-nine men under the near gate, three hundred days unpaid measured from the first of March, which is the eighty-fifth day of this flood, on the three hundred and eighty-fifth day of this flood, all three numbers in the sentence that prints the figure, said out loud on a road in the open by a man who does not trade rates, and a ruled line at the bottom of a column that is still ruled and still empty.
6. **Chapter 0386 — no change.** A sheet of road put down on the bottom step of nine steps, in the open, in front of one person, and refused standing and before the fourth crease was open, and the reason given out loud by the woman who put it there, which is that she did not ask whether the other woman wanted it and did it anyway and the woman she did it to is the one who has to carry the having done it. The second of the four creases had gone soft and the fourth is the one nobody has read.
7. **Chapter 0387 — two sentences.** The gate at the end of the lane was barred at the seventh hour, which is before the man of about twenty-five arrived at the eighth and before the woman of about fifty came out at the ninth: **the bar goes across at about the tenth now, and the hour is the one this manuscript already uses.** And the stallman had *chosen a wall over a board of boards*, which is the only occurrence of that phrase in forty files and reads as a slip: **it is a wall over a board, which is his second handle and the thing he says out loud seven lines later.** The man of about twenty-five still put his hand flat on the softest part of the shape and took it off again; the man of about thirty-four still said out loud that he has a piece of chalk he has not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month and is not going to write on it, and that the reason is that he has not got anything of his own to put there; the woman of about fifty still said four words to a post and they were a date.
8. **Chapter 0388 — no change.** The seventh sheet ruled in one room over a market by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, seven lines, nothing at the top of it, no name, not carried down nine steps and not sent, and a room agreeing out loud that what it is for is not going to be written down.
9. **Chapter 0389 — no change.** The seventh and last sheet, four days, unsigned, nothing at the top of it, to a place and not to a house, for the room on the seventy-eighth day of the Bare Month, and the woman who wrote it saying out loud that that is the seventh, that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood, and that a person who is going to write one more ought to say out loud that it is the last one while there is still somebody in the room to hear it. The carrier has never opened one.
10. **Chapter 0390 — no change.** A room over a market spent a whole Saturday doing nothing and nobody in it decided that, the woman of about twenty-one was asked a different question by a different person and answered it, and the room agreed out loud that what the three of them are carrying is not going to be written down, and the reason given is that that is the only way any of them is going to be able to say afterwards that they were not sent. A blank is the only thing in four hundred miles a return cannot be filled in from.

**AND THE FIGURES THAT MOVED WHEN THESE SIX WERE REPAIRED, WITH THE FIRST EDITION BESIDE THE SECOND, because a repair pass that moves prose and publishes one set of figures afterwards is the same fault a third time.** The bolded share of Chapter 0382 **9.27 to 8.73** and Chapter 0383's **19.04 to 19.01**, the unweighted mean **13.03 to 12.97**, the weighted mean **13.0 to 12.92**, the words **15,602 to 15,710**, the paragraphs **221 to 223**, the chorus frame's two denominators **44.2 to 44.6** and **22.1 to 22.3**, the long sentences **177 to 179** and Chapter 0382's own **19 to 21**, the longest run inside the ten **38 to 37** and now between Chapters 0384 and 0390, the run between 0381 and 0390 **38 to 34** and now a different frame, the run between 0381 and 0384 **36 to 24**, the barrows in Chapter 0382 **28 to 30** and the boards in Chapter 0387 **26 to 25**, and the declared string *about four people in* **at zero in six of the ten to at zero in five of them**, which was a wrong sentence in a record and not a wrong chapter. **The range of the bolded share, the longest bolded turn at eighty-eight words, the paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker at zero in all ten, the exchange at fifteen of thirty-two, the longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs at three, the chorus frame's counts at ten and five with a maximum of one per `---` section, the counting device at once in 0381 and once in 0390, the forty-three weekday tokens, and *passage*, *privilege*, *holder* and *volume* at zero across the ten files are all unchanged and were all measured again on the files.**

**AND WHAT NONE OF THE SIX REPAIRS COULD CHANGE.** Adrian Vale performed no working, opened no threshold, is not offered a workway, is aged nowhere, and is named in Chapter 0381 and in no other of the ten, in any form, and he is not in Chapter 0392 either — that is the next batch's chapter and `outline/volume-08.md` §4 is the cast rule. The two women of twenty-nine are never in one room. *About two hundred rooms* is at zero across all ten files. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the man who is right about it in this volume is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393. Nobody counted the days since the fourth of the four said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth. The ninth line did not move. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything. The town four hundred miles inland was not entered. The wall said nothing on all ten days and this batch spends no panel, because the volume's one panel is fixed to day 393. **The volume's own name is still a man's answer spoken once in Chapter 0357 and written down in no file, and this note does not have it either.**

**THE NEXT PHASE AFTER THIS BATCH IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and it is a BATCH and it is the LAST batch of Volume 08, and it is not the close.** It writes Chapters 0391 to 0400, days 391 to 400, from a Sunday to a Tuesday, and it carries the resolution, the new question and the last image: day 391 is when three people go down the road with a barrow each, day 393 is the climax and the volume's one panel, day 395 is the register line with a day on it and nothing under the day, day 396 is the new question asked and not answered, and day 400 is the fence and the road going away under it. **It carries three repairs from this review and a writer is told about them: the register and the slates are in 0392 and the folded sheet of road is in 0399 and not in 0392, because prohibition 13 will not let both women of twenty-nine be in the one chapter that has Adrian Vale in it; the boards and the bare wall are in 0397 and 0400, which is what `outline/volume-08.md` §20.10 says and what the prompt's own item 5 already assumed; and the man of about thirty-nine has a room count and it runs.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005 — Chapters 0391 to 0400, days 391 to 400 — THE AUTHORITATIVE BLOCK

**Ten chapters, one chapter to one day, no double day, from a Sunday to a Tuesday, the Bare Month days seventy-six to eighty-five. The volume's own name is spoken once in Chapter 0357 and is in no file and this block does not have it.**

1. **Chapter 0391** — a Sunday, the seventy-sixth day of the Bare Month. Three barrows go out past the last named house, one of them the man of about thirty-eight's and two of them belonging to nobody since before the water, and the three with them are the man of about twenty-five, the man of about forty-eight and the woman of about twenty-one. A barrow with a nail standing up out of the hub is noticed and not mentioned; a rise of about nine hundred paces is gone up four times; nobody is looking for anything; the town four hundred miles inland is not going to be seen by anybody on that road; the page the copy was made from is not being fetched and a party of four is four; nobody gets stronger; the woman of about twenty-one declines to say again what she said on Saturday. **A journey is not a hunt and this chapter says so on the page in a mouth and not in a plan.**
2. **Chapter 0392** — a Monday, the seventy-seventh. The room over a market. The form is going out on the seventy-eighth and has nothing at the top of it, and a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register says out loud that a form with a heading and four places and nothing at the top of it is a piece of paper. Adrian Vale is at the far end of the bench reading the register, which is not his, with the third column empty, and nobody asks him anything. **The man of about thirty-four says a woman's name out loud in daylight in front of about nine people, says the price of it before he does it, and nobody writes it down.** The second slate is on the shelf with a date on it and nothing under it. The other of the two women of about twenty-nine is named as not going to be in that room.
3. **Chapter 0393** — a Tuesday, the seventy-eighth. **THE CLIMAX AND THE VOLUME'S ONE PANEL.** The return goes out on its date with a name at the top of it, four places under a heading, a district with nowhere to start, about four hundred and forty in the second place in the man of about thirty-eight's own hand, a rota empty on purpose and a share that is a household's and stays empty. **The man of about thirty-eight came up the nine steps uninvited. The man of about thirty-nine is right in one turn of speech and nobody told him he was wrong and nobody told him he was right. The wall says one thing, in a plain block of its own, giving a rule and not an answer, and the man of about thirty-eight refuses it in six words in about four seconds and nobody improves on them and nobody thanks him, and the page goes anyway.** A day the wall says something on is not a silence.
4. **Chapter 0394** — a Wednesday, the seventy-ninth. The wharf boards. The page is under a stone off a doorstep on the only flat thing for four streets. The man of about thirty-eight says **about four hundred and forty** out loud in the open and says the other one is not going on the form and does not print it, and says out loud that the price of a name at the top of a page is not paid by the woman whose name it is and not by the woman who wrote it and is paid by the man who fills in the rest of it correctly and has nowhere to put what he knows, and that in about nine years a clerk nine hundred miles off is going to ask him where the figure came from. The man of about thirty-nine stands about nine feet off with his board under his arm and says nothing. Nobody thanks anybody.
5. **Chapter 0395** — a Thursday, the eightieth. **THE REGISTER LINE.** A woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register rules a line, puts **the seventy-sixth day of the Bare Month** in the first column, leaves the second column empty and leaves the third column empty on purpose, and enters nothing else at all. Both days stand in one sentence with the count. **No name goes under that day. Nobody goes near the shelf, and the second slate stays on it with a date on it and nothing under it.** The man of about thirty-four says the price of the empty second column out loud before it happens. A man of about fifty-seven looks at the line and is not asked.
6. **Chapter 0396** — a Friday, the eighty-first. **THE NEW QUESTION, ASKED AND NOT ANSWERED.** The register-keeper is not in the room, so the two women of twenty-nine are not in one. The woman of about sixty-nine, with an empty tin she has carried a mile, asks **who a fence is for**, to the room and not to anybody, and then the whole of it. **Nobody answered it. About four of the nine had an answer and about four said nothing, and the answer four of them had was a fence, and the chapter says so in the next line.** The man of about fifty-seven and the man of about sixty are both in that room and neither is asked. The last question she asked was on the thirty-first day of the Bare Month, a Thursday, which is fifty days back, and it was not answered either.
7. **Chapter 0397** — a Saturday, the eighty-second. The shut road, the fence at **sixteen posts and eleven withies** bound with the same cord that is on the head of a mallet in a handcart, and the man of about fifty-nine who walks a four-hundred-mile road and has never seen anybody on it. The man of about sixty says out loud that nobody in four hundred miles has ever asked him what it is for and that he has begun to want to know and is not going to find out. The man of about fifty-seven says the arrangement is not going to be settled this flood by either of them. Down in a lane, **a wall with the shape of nine boards gone off it and about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it**, the boards having come off it on the thirty-second day of that month, a Friday, and **no barrow of boards, which does not exist after that day.** The woman of about fifty writes a date on a post and says it to the wood.
8. **Chapter 0398** — a Sunday, the eighty-third. **RECOVERY.** A market roof that came down in a squall on the sixty-second day of the Bare Month goes back up on a morning that is not wet, about nine people, no hurry. The man of about thirty-four takes a pole in his left hand and nobody says anything about it; the man of about thirty-eight watches and does not say that he knows, as on the sixty-seventh day of that month. The woman of about thirty-four says a number at the head of her own stall and will not tell him what it was, and neither of them has ever written theirs down. **The one thing in this flood that has come back up is a roof.**
9. **Chapter 0399** — a Monday, the eighty-fourth. Tamsin Quill on the wharf boards. **She takes the folded sheet of road out of her apron at about the ninth hour, with nobody near her, opens it along the four creases she has always opened it along, and about nine places are on it and she says none of them and points at none of them; she folds it the same way and puts it back and does not offer it to anybody.** The man of about thirty-eight does not look at it and is not asked to. The man of about thirty-one says out loud that he has never asked anybody what they are carrying. **The space where a man is not at the far end of a bench is named as a space and not as an apology, and the consent fracture is not mended, not softened, not asked about and not explained.**
10. **Chapter 0400** — a Tuesday, the eighty-fifth. **THE LAST IMAGE.** A shut road, **a gate with two bars in it and the second one on no roll at all**, a lane below with **a wall with nine boards gone off it and about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it**, a fence of **sixteen posts and eleven withies**, the man of about fifty-nine with his barrow on the low side of it, the man of about fifty-seven and the man of about sixty and neither of them asked anything, a party of four is four, and **three barrows on the road going away under the fence.** **None of the four round figures that `outline/volume-08.md` §14.6 places on this day is printed in it, and the chapter does not mention any of the four anchors, and no meaning is attached to the eighty-fifth.** The last line is a thing a reader can see and not a sentence about a thing.

**AND THE FIGURES THAT MOVED WHILE THESE TEN WERE WRITTEN, WITH THE FIRST EDITION BESIDE THE SECOND, because a repair pass that moves prose and publishes one set of figures afterwards is the same fault a third time.** The bolded share of Chapter 0393 **2.52 to 9.76** and of Chapter 0400 **0.00 to 5.39**, the unweighted mean **11.71 to 13.69**, the weighted mean **13.0 to 13.91**, the words **16,760 to 16,516**, the paragraphs **237 to 233**, the chorus frame on (A) **one in 13.2 to one in 58.2** and on (B) **one in 4.4 to one in 16.6**, the long sentences **163 to 170**, and the longest bolded turn **137 words to 90**. **The two figures at zero were Chapters 0398 and 0400 on the first pass and they were at zero because neither chapter had a speech in it at all, which the exchange instrument also read as zero plain speeches, and one exchange was added to each.** The chorus frame's repair removed forty instances and took four paragraphs with it and added none. **AND A THIRD PASS, WHICH WAS A REVIEW REPAIR AND NOT A WRITING PASS, AND WHICH MOVED FOUR CHAPTERS' BOLDED SHARE DOWNWARD AND ONE PARAGRAPH COUNT AND EVERY DENOMINATOR THAT SAT ON IT:** Chapters 0392 **17.97 to 17.90**, 0395 **17.11 to 16.89**, 0397 **16.69 to 16.43** and 0400 **5.40 to 5.39**, the range **5.40–17.97 to 5.39–17.90**, the words **16,455 to 16,516**, the paragraphs **235 to 233**, and the object table's fourteen rows re-measured on marker sets that are now printed, of which seven reproduced and seven moved and are withdrawn. The prose repairs that moved the figures are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §9 items 11 and 12 and are the geography in Chapters 0397 and 0400, the columns in Chapter 0395 and the date in Chapter 0392.

**AND WHAT NONE OF THE REPAIRS COULD CHANGE.** Adrian Vale performed no working, opened no threshold, is offered no workway, is aged nowhere, and is named in Chapter 0392 and in no other of the ten, in any form. The two women of twenty-nine are never in one room. *About two hundred rooms* is at zero across all ten files. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the man who is right about it is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that is his only appearance in the volume. Nobody counted the days since the fourth of the four said no, at any value. The ninth line did not move. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything and **the man of about fifty-seven was not asked where the fence is, was not told and was not defeated, and the new question went to a room and not to him.** The town four hundred miles inland was not entered and the page the copy was made from was not fetched. The wall said nothing on nine of the ten days and the volume's one panel is spent on the tenth. **The Bare Month did not end, no chapter of the ten printed a day after the eighty-fifth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and every one of the ten names the month in full at least once.** **The volume's own name is still a man's answer spoken once in Chapter 0357 and written down in no file, and this note does not have it either, and neither does the batch record.**

**THE NEXT PHASE AFTER THIS BATCH IS `workspace/volume-08/close/PROMPT.md`, and it is the VOLUME 08 CLOSE and not a batch.** It writes no chapter, it takes the volume's last four decisions, it writes `state/volume-08-close.md`, and it hands on whatever follows. **Its handoff note is `workspace/volume-08/close/HANDOFF.md`, which is a note and not a prompt and decides nothing.**

# VOLUME 08 CLOSE — Chapters 0351 to 0400, days 351 to 400, ALL FIFTY — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**Volume 08 is fifty chapters on fifty days and every one of them was written, and the close reads all fifty and records them. This block is the volume's spine; the per-chapter entries for each batch are above it at their own length and are not restated here. `state/volume-08-close.md` is the record and this block is its index.**

**1. Chapter 0351 — day 351, a Tuesday, the thirty-sixth.** A form comes up the road in a satchel and is put on a table by a man who carries things for a living and does not read it, and the first notice of this volume goes out in a hand, unsigned, with nothing at the top of it, to a place and not to a house, for a room on day 355. *The volume's first page, and the first hand on a thing in the volume is a carrier's.*
**2. Chapter 0352 — day 352, a Wednesday, the thirty-seventh.** Tamsin Quill's folded sheet of road in an apron in a room over a yard, and **Adrian Vale in a room reading a page**, and the figure *about two hundred rooms* on a page in narration and in a woman's mouth.
**3. Chapter 0353 — day 353, a Thursday, the thirty-eighth.** The last three fence posts and the eleven withies, and the fence finished. *A Thursday, and it is on the page in this chapter.*
**4. Chapter 0354 — day 354, a Friday, the thirty-ninth.** A chalk that is not taken out, a board, the second notice.
**5. Chapter 0355 — day 355, a Saturday, the fortieth.** The return read out in the local language in a room in daylight with a date on the door, about nine people, a district, a population, a rota and a share, and **the finding that there is no place on it for the person the four places are about, and the fourth place is put in the room for the first time.** *Adrian Vale in a room reading a page.*
**6. Chapter 0356 — day 356, a Sunday, the forty-first.** A household of about nine, a door she is not asked to open, and a refusal standing on a step before anybody asked.
**7. Chapter 0357 — day 357, a Monday, the forty-second.** **THE DAY THIS VOLUME IS NAMED AFTER AND THE DAY ITS NAME IS NOT PRINTED IN ANY FILE.** A man of about thirty-eight asked what the column is for and answers, in his own words, and the answer is the name of this volume and is in no file in this repository. *Adrian Vale in a room reading a page.*
**8. Chapter 0358 — day 358, a Tuesday, the forty-third.** Nine hours in a room in which nobody asks anybody anything.
**9. Chapter 0359 — day 359, a Wednesday, the forty-fourth.** A sheet on a wall and a man who does not stop at a gate, and the third notice.
**10. Chapter 0360 — day 360, a Thursday, the forty-fifth.** A deed, a pinned copy, a form, a man's rope on a chair, and *about two hundred rooms* on Adrian Vale in narration. *The pressure lands.*
**11. Chapter 0361 — day 361, a Friday, the forty-sixth.** A man who walks the four-hundred-mile road, a hurdle he made and a bar he replaced.
**12. Chapter 0362 — day 362, a Saturday, the forty-seventh.** A notice four days, and a number said by somebody else, and the price of it.
**13. Chapter 0363 — day 363, a Sunday, the forty-eighth.** Three of the four in one room and a question about a column, and nobody in the room marking the moment.
**14. Chapter 0364 — day 364, a Monday, the forty-ninth.** Two men in front of a finished fence and the counts said out loud and no question. **One of the three sentences this close routed: `chapter-0364.md:3` says *above this city*.**
**15. Chapter 0365 — day 365, a Tuesday, the fiftieth.** A bare wall with a shape going off it, a second bar on no roll, and two not-askings.
**16. Chapter 0366 — day 366, a Wednesday, the fifty-first.** A notice four days, a task, and a condition stated first.
**17. Chapter 0367 — day 367, a Thursday, the fifty-second.** A nib ground narrow, a window that shuts, and two people not saying so. **The man of about forty-eight says *about two hundred rooms* in a mouth, and the nine that goes with it.**
**18. Chapter 0368 — day 368, a Friday, the fifty-third.** Three barrows on wet boards in a wind and a figure nobody answered, and **two hundred days measured from the hundred and sixty-eighth, with the anchor in the sentence.**
**19. Chapter 0369 — day 369, a Saturday, the fifty-fourth.** A wharf with a pile on it, four hundred and forty, and nothing sent. *The man of about thirty-one and the figure in narration.*
**20. Chapter 0370 — day 370, a Sunday, the fifty-fifth.** A room doing half of a task that is not its own, and a decision not to send.
**21. Chapter 0371 — day 371, a Monday, the fifty-sixth.** A sheet with nothing at the top of it and the price of that said out loud, and the sixth notice. *Adrian Vale in a room reading a page.*
**22. Chapter 0372 — day 372, a Tuesday, the fifty-seventh.** A man who fills three of four places four times a year and has never once filled the fourth.
**23. Chapter 0373 — day 373, a Wednesday, the fifty-eighth.** A sheet unfolded once in the open and folded again the same way, and a crease gone soft.
**24. Chapter 0374 — day 374, a Thursday, the fifty-ninth.** A room arranging a bench and deciding out loud not to say what it is for, and **the man of about thirty-four says *about two hundred rooms* in a speech paragraph that carries no descriptor in it.**
**25. Chapter 0375 — day 375, a Friday, the sixtieth. THE MIDPOINT.** A question asked in a room — two words, once, to the woman of about twenty-one — and nobody in it asking a second one, and the man of about forty-eight saying the figure again with the nine.
**26. Chapter 0376 — day 376, a Saturday, the sixty-first.** A yard, the order nine things are done in, and a man who is not told whether he was right. *Adrian Vale, and the one exception the decision of record names.*
**27. Chapter 0377 — day 377, a Sunday, the sixty-second.** A roof down by hand in a squall and a number handed back to the woman who owns it. **The lowest bolded share in the volume.**
**28. Chapter 0378 — day 378, a Monday, the sixty-third.** Two four-place forms counted out loud in a room and not put side by side, eight places, and not one the same as any of the other seven. **The only chapter of the volume that may do this.**
**29. Chapter 0379 — day 379, a Tuesday, the sixty-fourth.** Nine rows at the back of a register that nobody can date, and a room deciding not to copy them.
**30. Chapter 0380 — day 380, a Wednesday, the sixty-fifth.** A sheet put on a bench in a room with about nine people in it, two nines that are not the same nine, and a refusal.
**31. Chapter 0381 — day 381, a Thursday, the sixty-sixth.** A price for not asking, said out loud by the person who pays it, and a page in the inside of a coat nobody asks about. *Adrian Vale in a room reading a page.*
**32. Chapter 0382 — day 382, a Friday, the sixty-seventh.** Three barrows at the top of some boards, two of them nobody's, and a hazel mallet brought up a hill nobody sent for.
**33. Chapter 0383 — day 383, a Saturday, the sixty-eighth.** One question asked in the open on a wharf, a number said in the man's own mouth, and a second number not said.
**34. Chapter 0384 — day 384, a Sunday, the sixty-ninth.** The four places under a heading read out loud, and a share that is a household's, and a column that goes out empty and correct.
**35. Chapter 0385 — day 385, a Monday, the seventieth. THE TRAP DAY.** Thirty-nine of forty under a gate, **three hundred days with the anchor named twice in the sentence**, and a ruled line still empty.
**36. Chapter 0386 — day 386, a Tuesday, the seventy-first.** A page offered in the open and refused, and the not-asking that went with it.
**37. Chapter 0387 — day 387, a Wednesday, the seventy-second.** A wall with a shape going off it, a piece of chalk that has not been out, and two men who did not use the wall between them.
**38. Chapter 0388 — day 388, a Thursday, the seventy-third.** The seventh sheet ruled and not carried down nine steps.
**39. Chapter 0389 — day 389, a Friday, the seventy-fourth. THE SEVENTH AND LAST NOTICE**, four days, unsigned, nothing at the top of it, to a place, and nobody opens it.
**40. Chapter 0390 — day 390, a Saturday, the seventy-fifth.** A Saturday on which nothing happened, a room agreeing that what three people carry will not be written down, and **the man of about thirty-nine's counter on a page — eleven in a fortnight — and no room count printed for him in any chapter after this one.**
**41. Chapter 0391 — day 391, a Sunday, the seventy-sixth. THE THREE GO.** Three people down a road out of this city with a barrow each and nothing to fetch, and a party of four is four.
**42. Chapter 0392 — day 392, a Monday, the seventy-seventh.** A woman's name said out loud in daylight in a room with a date on the door and written down nowhere, and the price said before the doing. **Adrian Vale in a room reading a register that is not his, and the last of the seven.**
**43. Chapter 0393 — day 393, a Tuesday, the seventy-eighth. THE CLIMAX.** A page with a name at the top of it goes out on its date, correct, about the wrong people; the man of about thirty-nine is right in one turn of speech and is thanked by nobody; **the volume's one panel, refused in about four seconds in six words**, and the page goes anyway.
**44. Chapter 0394 — day 394, a Wednesday, the seventy-ninth.** A figure said out loud on a wharf and the working behind it that has no place on the form.
**45. Chapter 0395 — day 395, a Thursday, the eightieth. THE RESOLUTION.** A day in a register with nothing under it, and nobody putting a pen near a shelf. **The day on that line is day 391.**
**46. Chapter 0396 — day 396, a Friday, the eighty-first. THE NEW QUESTION, ASKED AND NOT ANSWERED.** Who a fence is for, asked to a room by the woman of about sixty-nine, about four of the nine having an answer and about four of them saying nothing, and the two men it is about in the room and neither of them asked. **Two of this chapter's three sentences about the shut road's place are routed by the close.**
**47. Chapter 0397 — day 397, a Saturday, the eighty-second.** A fence finished a month ago, a man who has begun to want to know what his own work is for, and a wall with nine boards gone off it.
**48. Chapter 0398 — day 398, a Sunday, the eighty-third.** A market roof that goes back up on a morning that is not wet, and a hand that closes.
**49. Chapter 0399 — day 399, a Monday, the eighty-fourth.** A sheet of road taken out of an apron and folded the same way, and a space where a man is not.
**50. Chapter 0400 — day 400, a Tuesday, the eighty-fifth. THE LAST IMAGE.** A gate with two bars, a wall with nine boards gone off it, a fence of sixteen posts and eleven withies, three barrows going away under it, and a man who did not go after them. **None of the four round figures that fall on day 400 is printed in it, and the chapter does not mention any of the four anchors.**

**AND WHAT NONE OF THE FIFTY DID, which a later writer has to know is still untouched: the word on the second slate is at zero in all fifty files; the day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the man who is right about it is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that is his only appearance in the volume; nobody counted the days since the fourth of the four said no, at any value, in any of the fifty; the ninth line did not move; the keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything and the man of about fifty-seven was not asked where the fence is, was not told and was not defeated; the town four hundred miles inland was not entered and the page the copy was made from was not fetched; the two women of about twenty-nine were never in one room; the words *passage*, *privilege*, *holder* and *delegation* are at zero across all fifty files; nobody got stronger, Adrian Vale is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performed no working, opened no threshold, named the other world not once and was offered no workway and asked for none; and the wall said nothing on forty-nine of the fifty days and its one panel is spent. THE BARE MONTH DID NOT END, NO CHAPTER OF THE FIFTY PRINTED A DAY AFTER THE EIGHTY-FIFTH, SAID THE MONTH WAS NEARLY OVER, SAID WHAT IT IS A MONTH OF, OR ATTACHED *LENGTH*, *LONG* OR *SHORT* TO IT, AND EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTY NAMES THE MONTH IN FULL AT LEAST ONCE.**

**THE VOLUME'S OWN NAME IS A MAN'S ANSWER SPOKEN ONCE IN CHAPTER 0357 AND WRITTEN DOWN IN NO FILE, and this block does not have it either, and neither does the batch record, nor the close record, nor the series outline, nor the bible, nor the next phase's prompt.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE AFTER THIS CLOSE IS `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md`, and it is a VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE and not a batch and not a close. It writes no chapter, there is no Chapter 0401, and this phase wrote none. It decides the line and audits it, the cast and the instrument, and it works inside `bible/power-system.md` §51, which is the calendar for days 401 to 450, and the Bare Month does not end inside Volume 09 and §51 gives it no length.**

## VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE — and this block wins over everything above it

**A Volume 09 outline phase has run. IT WROTE NO CHAPTER. There is no Chapter 0401 and this phase did not write one, and there is no Chapter 0402 through 0410 either. What is below is a PLAN and not a record of prose, and a plan is a plan and the page is the fact.**

**THE FIFTY CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 09, DAYS 401 TO 450, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, NO DOUBLE DAY, IN FIVE MOVEMENTS OF TEN, from the plan at `outline/volume-09.md` and the decisions in `outline/series.md`. Not one of these fifty exists as prose and every entry below is a card, not a summary.**

**MOVEMENT ONE, DAYS 401 TO 410, Chapters 0401 to 0410, Wednesday to Friday. The sheet arrives and is read out.**
- **0401, day 401, a Wednesday, the eighty-sixth.** A second sheet comes up the road in a satchel and is put on a table by a man who carries things for a living and does not open it. He has carried three such things in two volumes and opened none. **He says one true thing out loud about why he is not opening it and the reason is his.** *A card, not a chapter.*
- **0402, day 402, a Thursday, the eighty-seventh.** A room over a market, a slate on a shelf with a date on it and nothing under it, and a man of about fifty-seven standing in the room for about four hours with a length of new rope over his arm and not being asked anything. **Adrian Vale is in this chapter because he is reading a page, and he watches a man not be asked and that is the whole of what he does.**
- **0403, day 403, a Friday, the eighty-eighth.** A man in a very good coat with a document inside the coat and no local language, and the boards of a salt wharf, and the fact that the same round has carried a page with a name at the top of it and a page with a place for a name in it. **Neither he nor the man on the boards says anything to the other one.**
- **0404, day 404, a Saturday, the eighty-ninth.** Tamsin Quill in a kitchen and then on the wharf boards, saying once, in about nine words and in her own mouth, that a sheet which says where a line runs is the plainest instrument of a claim this flood has produced. **The consent fracture is visible and is not mended and nobody apologises.**
- **0405, day 405, a Sunday, the ninetieth. THE BATCH EVENT.** The sheet is read out in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, about nine people. **A place, a line, a day, and a person, in that order, and the third of them is a person, and it is the first page in this flood that has ever asked anybody to put themselves on it.** Nobody fills anything.
- **0406 to 0410** are a room that has just heard a person and has not answered, a man who will not use a piece of chalk, a man working boards who is right in four seconds and is thanked by nobody, a woman who has said she will find something out and has not, and a barrow put down on the low side of a fence at the top of a shut road out of this city with three people on that road going away from him.

**MOVEMENT TWO, DAYS 411 TO 420.** A household is asked. **A woman of about fifty-four is put on a page on day 420 by being asked where her line is, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, and that is the volume's only new person and the whole of her first appearance.** Her second handle — a length of blue thread tied round her wrist — is invented on that page.

**MOVEMENT THREE, DAYS 421 TO 430, AND IT CARRIES THE MIDPOINT AND THE NAME.** **Day 425, a Saturday, the hundred and tenth, the nearer of the two middle days: a room decides out loud, naming the cost first, that the third place is left empty, and there is no notice in this volume and therefore no four days of cooling, and the chapter says so once in narration. Day 428, a Tuesday, the hundred and thirteenth: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall is asked what a line is, in a room with a date on the door, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, and what he says in four to eight words is the name of this volume and is on that page and in no file.**

**MOVEMENT FOUR, DAYS 431 TO 440, AND IT CARRIES THE CLIMAX.** **Day 432: four lines are drawn and the man of about fifty-seven reads one out loud because it is in front of him and does not say it is his and is not asked. Day 438, a Friday, the hundred and twenty-third, THE CLIMAX: the sheet goes out on its date with four lines and nothing in the third column, the man of about thirty-nine is right in four seconds and is thanked by nobody, and the wall says one rule and the man of about thirty-four refuses it in four seconds and what it costs him is his own ninth line. Day 440, a Sunday: the sheet comes back with one of the four lines moved, and nobody traces it and nobody will ever be asked about it.**

**MOVEMENT FIVE, DAYS 441 TO 450. Day 443: the register carries a second line with a day on it and nothing under it, the day being day 438 and the day of the entering being day 443, and both are named in the chapter. Day 445, a Friday, the hundred and thirtieth: the woman of about sixty-nine asks a third question to the room and nobody answers it. Day 450, a Wednesday, the hundred and thirty-fifth, THE LAST IMAGE: the third column, folded, empty, with a date in chalk on the door above it, and a road out of this city with three barrows on it, fifty-nine days out, measured from day 391 with the anchor named in the same sentence.**

**AND WHAT NONE OF THE FIFTY WILL DO, which a later writer has to know is still untouched and which is why the plan is a plan and not a licence: nobody gets stronger; the Bare Month does not end and is given no length; the word on the second slate is on no page; the day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the two men who are right about a column are right in about four seconds and are thanked by nobody; nobody counts the days since the fourth of the four said no, at any value; the ninth line does not move and its author is not thanked; the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is and is not told and is not defeated and does not use his rope; the mark in the field is not measured, dug, counted or probed; the three on the road are not followed and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered; the keeper at a shut gate is not asked anything and is not in the volume; the girl of seventeen is in no chapter; and the shut road is out of this city and not above it.**

**THE VOLUME'S OWN NAME IS A MAN'S ANSWER SPOKEN ONCE IN CHAPTER 0428 AND WRITTEN DOWN IN NO FILE, and this block does not have it, and neither does the outline, the batch cards, the series outline, the bible, the state files, or the prompt this phase created.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE AFTER THIS OUTLINE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and it is a BATCH and not an outline. It writes Chapters 0401 to 0410, days 401 to 410, ten chapters of finished prose, and it decides nothing.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — Chapters 0411 to 0420, days 411 to 420 — and this block wins over every block above it

**Chapter 0411 — One Of The Four Lines Is Going To Be Hers.** Day 411, a Saturday, the ninety-sixth day of the Bare Month. The room over a market, about nine people, the day in chalk on the door, and the sheet on the table with a heading and four places ruled under it and a crease pressed down the third of them and nothing in any of the four. **The woman of about fifty-two comes up the nine steps, sits at the end of the bench furthest from the door, reads the page from where she is sitting without being given it and without being asked to look at it, and says out loud, to the middle of the room, before anybody has said so, that there are going to be four lines on that page and one of the four is going to be hers — and that she has said it out loud first so that nobody in that room can stand there later on and say that she was asked.** Nobody asked her anything. Nobody told her she was right or that she was wrong. The register-keeper put her hand down beside the sheet and not on it. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall shifted a foot along the bench at the back without turning round. **Nothing was drawn, nothing was written, the register stayed shut on its shelf, and her door at the end of a lane was not knocked on that day and is not going to be.**

**Chapter 0412 — A Space At The Top Of Some Boards About The Length Of A Barrow.** Day 412, a Sunday, the ninety-seventh. The boards of a salt wharf at the bottom of the city, wet at the bottom end in the morning, and two men on them and nobody else. **The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf works the boards with his tally-board under his arm and his knife in his hand, and there has been no barrow at the top of them since day 391, when a man of about twenty-five came up out of the low ground with his hand out and took it away without one word being said on either side.** The man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board comes down at about the eighth hour with his own board under his arm and stands at the bottom of them, and neither of them says anything for a while, and the man of about thirty-eight says out loud that the barrow went down the four-hundred-mile road on the Sunday it went, that he gave it away in about a quarter of an hour, that he did not say why and is not going to, and that nobody in this city has asked him about it once. **Nobody told him he was right and nobody asked him why.** Nine sentences go between them across the morning and none of the nine is about the sheet on a table in a room over a market. At the end of it the man of about thirty-nine lays his own board down, the man of about thirty-eight cuts one length off it without being asked how long, it comes off about a forearm long, the first man says he did not say how long and was not asked, the second says he has got it too long and will have to saw it himself, and the man of about thirty-nine goes up off the wharf with two boards under his arm instead of one. **The space at the top of the stack is where it was.**

**Chapter 0413 — The Page Came Out Of A Fold On The Third Of The Four Places.** Day 413, a Monday, the ninety-eighth. The room over a market, and **the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says out loud that the page was folded twice, that one of the two folds is the one down the third of the four places and the other is along the top, and that somebody nine hundred miles off put that sheet together so that the third of the four places fell on a fold.** He says he is not asking for anything and that he is not going to draw the second one and nobody in that room should let him. A woman at the far end of the bench says a crease is a crease and it goes when a page is opened, and he answers that it is not a fold, that it is not going to go, and that a crease pressed hard enough with a bone is a line somebody drew without a pen. **Nobody in that room worked out how a line gets onto a page in four hundred miles, the register-keeper put the lump of chalk a half inch further from the edge of the table, and about nine people went down nine steps by the fourth hour with a thing they had heard worked out and nothing on any page in this city to put it in.** No hand opened the sheet, folded it, wrote on it or turned it over.

**Chapter 0414 — A Book With Three Columns In It And One Line.** Day 414, a Tuesday, the ninety-ninth. The room over a market, and **Adrian Vale is in it and is asked for nothing and asks for nothing, and he reads the register where it lies without lifting the book, without turning a page and without putting a hand on the frame of the shelf it lives on.** The book has three columns ruled in it; the first has a day in it and nothing under the day, the second is empty, and the third has been empty on purpose for fifty days. **He finds the margin worn pale in a curve about the width of a thumb beside the third column and not beside the other two, and says one true thing about the paper in front of about nine people and stops one step short of it, and nobody in that room asked him for the rest of it and nobody thanked him.** The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf comes up at about the ninth hour with his tally-board and his knife, is told he is not going to be asked to write anything down, turns the tally-board so its face is away from the room, and gives one figure to the room — about four hundred and forty, on eleven miles of flats — and nobody asks him a second question about it. **The book goes back on the shelf it lives on, and the stair is colder than the room had been, and the knife is still blunt.**

**Chapter 0415 — A Man With A Document He Cannot Read Out Loud To Anybody.** Day 415, a Wednesday, **the hundredth day of the Bare Month, and it is a day like any other and nothing was said about it by anybody.** The room over a market, the door shut for the first hour it has been shut on a working morning in this flood. **The man of about forty-one in a very good coat comes up the nine steps, puts his hat on the bench, does not take his coat off, and says out loud, in his own language, for the second time in this city and without being asked, that he does not have the local one — and says it twice rather than once because nobody had said it for him.** He takes a document out of the front of his coat, lays it across his own knee, keeps a palm flat on the whole of it, reads it, puts it back and buttons the coat. **He says out loud that he has been in most of these rooms and has not been in one in which anything got decided, and nobody asks him where he has been and nobody in that room offered to find anybody who had the language, and the door is shut for an hour and a half and then it is not.** The sheet stays on the table with nothing in any of the four places.

**Chapter 0416 — Two Of The Four Places Can Be Answered By Anybody And One Of Them Cannot.** Day 416, a Thursday, the hundred and first. The room over a market, and **the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register counts the four places under the sheet's heading out loud in front of about nine people: the first is a place and anybody in the room can write it, the fourth is a day and anybody in the room can write that too, and the second is a line, and a line is a thing a person can put down whether or not anybody is standing on either side of it.** She says it is a count and that she has made it, and **she names no day and does not say what any of the four is for.** The man of about thirty-four says he is not going to be the one that puts a mark on any of them. She puts her finger beside the third of the four places and not in it. **The man of about fifty-seven is in that room with the length of new rope over the back of his chair and is asked nothing, and the fence along the top of a shut road out of this city is sixteen willow posts with eleven withies, and the rope is still not used, and nobody in that room has ever asked him where the fence is.** Nobody in that room told the register-keeper she was right about her count.

**Chapter 0417 — A Squall Over A Market And A Wall That Washes Itself.** Day 417, a Friday, the hundred and second. A market at the bottom of the city in a squall at about the sixth hour. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall takes his own board out of the weather himself, twice, because the first time the wind came round the corner of the market and took the top of it.** The man of about twenty-seven stands at the end of the store with a bucket and a rag and his arm over his face and nothing over the board, **and the wall above the store's board — which he has washed every morning for about a month — is washed for him, in a quarter of an hour, by the sky, on a Friday, for a decision he made about a month ago.** The man of about thirty-four puts the bucket down in the doorway, puts his own coat over the other man's arm, gets the board and washes it from the top down under the eave, and **says out loud that he did not stand there and ask, and that he is not going to let it get about that a man washed a board he did not have to wash.** The man of about twenty-seven says nothing about it and goes back to his bucket. **Nobody in that market thanked either of them, the barrow at the end of the stall stood in the wet with nothing in it, and one wall was clean.**

**Chapter 0418 — Two Men At The Two Ends Of One Bench.** Day 418, a Saturday, the hundred and third. The room over a market, and **the man of about fifty-seven, who has not been asked one question in this flood, says out loud at about the ninth hour, to the man of about thirty-four at the other end of the same bench, that there is a first thing he could say to anybody who asked him and that he is not going to say it — and that he has said so out loud so that everybody in that room knows there is one and nobody can say afterwards that he led anybody up to it.** The man of about thirty-four answers it back without turning round, and neither of them thanked the other. **The length of new rope stays over the back of the chair it was on and is not used. The three that went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391, a Sunday, are named in the room and no elapsed count is printed for them, and nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road.** Nobody said anything.

**Chapter 0419 — The Page Came Up The Road And Has Been On A Table Every Morning Since.** Day 419, a Sunday, the hundred and fourth. The room over a market, and **the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says out loud, at the back of the room, unasked, that the man in the very good coat has been sitting ten feet from that page every morning this week and has not read one word of it, and that four of them have been talking about the third of the four places in front of him.** The woman of about twenty-nine says, to the table and not to anybody in it, that they worked it out about an hour ago and none of them said anything for about forty minutes — **and she is the first one in that room to admit the room had been wrong, and nobody thanked her for it.** The man of about thirty-four says that nobody in that room is going to arrange for him to have the language and nobody is going to say it to him.
