# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0005 — Chapters 0541 to 0550, days 541 to 550

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED. The full record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-11-batch-0005.md`, whose ten cards stand at its head and were written before Chapter 0541 existed.**

- **0541** — *A Bill Named By Somebody Who Did Not Pay It.* The first cost of this volume's decision is named out loud in that room by a woman near the far end of that bench who did not pay it, in two marked turns, and the woman who did pay it is sitting at the table with her hand on it and says nothing whatever about it. **She names what a room choosing one of four figures does to the three that stay and to the reader four hundred miles off, and she says there is no place in that room where a bill gets written down, and nothing was written down.** Nobody thanked her and nobody improved on her. The register was not opened, the shelf was not approached, and the chapter names almost nothing but the date on the door.
- **0542** — *A Wall Washed Once And The Figures Left Alone.* **THE DAY IS DIFFERENT AT THE END OF IT FROM THE START BECAUSE A MAN WASHED A WALL HE HAS NEVER WASHED.** He washed from the top down, a hand's width at a time, and stopped with the rag in his hand about two fingers above the figures, put the rag on the stone lip without touching the board or the figures, and put the board back on its two nails with all three sets of figures on it and none of them rubbed out. Nothing was entered anywhere that morning and nothing went out of that market, and the trough was over its lip for the first time in this flood. Nobody asked him why and nobody thanked him, and two people at stalls in that row said what the morning was and what he had not done, and neither of them said what it was worth.
- **0543** — *A Knot He Was Shown And A Different One Twice.* Adrian Vale ties a length of string in a knot round the near corner of the folded sheet that has been shut on that table, four times over the morning, and it is not the knot the man two stalls along ties on his trestle every day. He undoes it and does it again and gets the same different one. **The consequence is that the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living, coming up that stair to put his satchel on the bench, finds a loop of that string across the strap and has to take the whole of it in his hand and work at it with the flat of his thumb nail, and nobody in that room says a word to him while he does it and nobody asks him afterwards.** Adrian has no quoted word in the chapter, nobody in the room knows whose hands put the string there, and nobody thanked him.
- **0544** — *Seven Sheets And A Space About Two Fingers Wide.* The register comes off its shelf in the keeper's two hands, is stood open on the table, is not read out, has nothing written in it, and goes back on its shelf inside the hour; the column on the right of that page is empty from the top to the bottom and nobody in that room asks about it. Seven sheets that went out of this city in this flood are named in one plain sentence of narration and there is no eighth, and the four days are the time a room has to find out what it is being asked. A fence along the top of the road out past the last named house is named at sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and is not measured. **The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board says out loud, twice, that nobody in that room has asked him what was in a space about two fingers wide on the face of his board for nine years, and nobody asks him.**
- **0545** — *A Sheet Out Of A City In A Satchel With A Flap.* **THE VOLUME'S RESOLUTION AND NOTHING IS SOLVED BY IT.** The sheet comes off the table in two hands and goes into a satchel with a flap, the flap goes down and the strap goes round it twice and the buckle is not put on, and the man who carries things for a living takes it down that stair and out along the flags and does not open it and nobody in that room asked him to or ever will. The four forms are named once in narration and are never joined. The chapter says in one plain sentence that what has been achieved is that a figure of this city is outside this city and what has been lost is that a figure of this city is outside this city, and no mouth in that room improves on it or says either half out loud. **No document was defeated and nobody was rescued and nobody was defeated and nothing in this flood has an instrument for bringing a figure back.**
- **0546** — *Wet Flags And A Bucket Standing In The Middle Of A Row.* Adrian Vale works standing water off the flags with a rag and a bucket from the store towards the near gate, and the flags are not dry at the fourth hour and are not dry at the eighth, and the man who washes that board every morning was out on those flags before he was and put nothing on them and took nothing off them. **He leaves the bucket standing in the middle of the row and the man of about sixty has to lift the front of his handcart over it with both hands on the shafts and go on up that row with one wheel off the ground, and nobody in that row knew whose hands put it there.** The trough never left its spot, the wall above the board was not washed, no figure was put on that board, and nobody said which of the three sets of figures on it anybody should be reading.
- **0547** — *A Name Nobody In That Room Can Say.* **THE SECOND COST AND IT IS THE ONE THE VOLUME IS ABOUT, AND IT IS NAMED OUT LOUD BY A PERSON WHO IS NOT THE PERSON IT IS ABOUT.** A woman near the step says, in her own mouth, that the four came off eleven miles of flats on four mornings inside ten days, that the person who made them is not in that room and is not on anything in this city, that there is no place on the sheet for her and there never was one, and that the one that went out on the Sunday is right and that right is the last thing anybody four hundred miles off can do anything about. **Nobody in that room asks who made the figure, a man near the far end of the bench stands up in the middle of the room and does not ask and sits down again, and the person the cost is about has no name, no age, no number and no descriptor on any page of this manuscript and is not in that room.** Nobody thanked the woman who named it.
- **0548** — *One Man At A Window And A Question The Room Did Not Hear.* **THE VOLUME'S NEW QUESTION, ASKED A SECOND TIME, TO ONE MAN AND NOT TO A ROOM.** The man of about fifty-nine who walks the four-hundred-mile road comes up that stair with a hurdle he made and a bar he replaced and stands at the window. The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asks it beside him at a volume that carried about as far as the two of them. He does not answer it and the room does not hear it, and the chapter says so in the next line. **Nobody in that room thanked her for asking it or told her it was a good question or a bad one, nobody in that room asked him about the three who went down that road, and she says out loud afterwards that she is not going to say it better than it was said the last time it was said in that room.** The register was not opened and the second slate did not move.
- **0549** — *A Slate On A Sill And A Lump Of Chalk That Is Not His.* Adrian Vale carries a blank slate the length of the row and sets it face up on the sill of that store, takes the store's chalk out of its box, holds it, puts it back, and writes nothing on it, because the piece of chalk he would write a figure of this city with in his own hand is in the man of about thirty-four's inside breast pocket and is not coming out of it. **The consequence is that a woman with a stall in that row, coming to put her own board up on that sill, finds a slate there that was not there the day before and puts the foot of her board on it to stop the wind taking it.** The man of about thirty-four says two things about the two chalks and nobody asks him about either of them. No figure off any of the four mornings is printed in the chapter and no fifth morning exists.
- **0550** — *A Board Washed Clean And A Road With Three Barrows On It.* **THIS VOLUME'S LAST IMAGE, A FRIDAY, THE TWO HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE BARE MONTH.** The board comes off its two nails at the fourth hour and is washed and goes back on them with three sets of figures on it and none of the three rubbed out, and the washing stops at the top of it. The book on the shelf behind the bench is not opened and its middle column is full and the column beside it is empty. A fence at sixteen willow posts and eleven withies is named and not measured. **A man of about thirty-nine says out loud that one of those two figures came four hundred miles and the other was made in this city on a morning by a person, that they are of one kind of thing and are not the same thing, and that there is nothing in four hundred miles that will turn one of them round and stand it beside the other one, and nobody in that room asked him which one a person should be using.** Three barrows are a hundred and fifty-nine days out on that road, measured from the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood, and a party of four set out and one of the four said no before anybody asked her.

**AND THE FIGURES FOR THE TEN, on the definitions at the batch record §1.1 and §1.7: the bolded share per chapter **12.23, 11.64, 13.50, 9.78, 13.23, 5.89, 12.79, 9.15, 9.85 and 8.06**, a range of 5.89 to 13.50 at an unweighted mean of 10.62 on 11,518 words in 158 paragraphs; paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker **zero in all ten on both readings**; the exchange **seventeen plain and eleven answered**; the longest bolded turn **eighty-six words in Chapter 0547**; the longest run of speech paragraphs **two**; runs of forty words or more **zero, measured, after six were cut**; distinct sixteen-word strings shared with a file outside the batch **four hundred and seventy-one**, and shared between two files of the batch **two hundred and twenty-two**; descriptors **eight distinct, fifty-three occurrences, none new**; elapsed counts **one file, one figure, its anchor in the same sentence**.**

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0005 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED. The full account is `state/batch-summaries/volume-11-batch-0005.md` §7. Eleven passages in six chapters were recut, and no day, no scene, no person, no object, no figure and no decision changed in any of them. Not one word inside a bold mark was edited.**

- **0541** — **TWO FALSE COUNTS CUT.** The sheet is shut on that table with two folds in it and the first edition dated its being put down *two days ago*; it was folded and shut on the day before this chapter, in both places it stood, and both now read *the day before*. **Nothing else in the chapter moved.**
- **0542** — **UNCHANGED, AND IT IS THE CHAPTER THE THREE OTHERS HAVE TO AGREE WITH.** The wall above the store's board is washed in this chapter and only in this chapter, from the top down, stopped about two fingers above the figures, and the stone above that line is not the colour it was the morning before the water came. **The forty-two-word description of the morning wash that stood in Chapter 0550 word for word with this chapter's opening was cut from 0550 and stands here untouched, and it is the reason 0550 now opens differently.**
- **0543** — **THE WORST FAULT IN THE BATCH, CUT.** The chapter had one paragraph saying the sheet on that table held a figure of this city *and* that the figure had gone out of this city two days ago, on a Friday two days before the Sunday it went out on. **It now says the figure has not been outside this city and is lying on that table under the one window.** The knot, the string round the near corner, the thumbnail, the man of about thirty-one and Adrian Vale's want for that knot to be the knot he was shown all stand, and **Adrian Vale still has no quoted word in this chapter.**
- **0544** — **TWO THINGS.** **The middle column of the register is now said to be full from the head of it to the foot of it and no longer claims to be ruled into five lines**, which is a count the plan's own row does not carry; the five figures stand. **And the seven-notices paragraph, which shared forty-six words word for word with Chapter 0531, is recut**; the seven sheets, the eighth that does not exist, the four days and the fact that no person in that room said any of it out loud all stand, **and the four days is one of the four elapsed figures in the batch and names its own anchor.**
- **0545** — **UNCHANGED.** The sheet goes out of this city in a satchel with a flap on a Sunday, the four documents are named once in narration and are never joined, and the first one-place form is named as standing on a shelf behind that bench. **That last naming is published at the batch record §7.1 as a carry against a row that says the two forms are never in one room, and it is not repaired, because the rule is a close's to settle.**
- **0546** — **THREE THINGS.** **Adrian Vale is on the flags after the man of about twenty-seven, not before him**, which is what the plan's published want for this chapter says and what the chapter's own next paragraph has always said. **The closing paragraph no longer leaves a handcart two rows up the row with a wheel off the ground** after the chapter has already brought it back down and stopped it at the near gate; it is at the near gate with the shafts down and a man beside it. **And the sentence saying the cart is not one of the three barrows, which shared forty-three words with Chapter 0535, is recut**, and the cart, the coil of rope, the hazel mallet with its head bound in cord, the three barrows and the four hundred miles all stand.
- **0547** — **FOUR THINGS, AND IT IS THE CHAPTER THAT TOOK THE MOST.** **Both marked turns now have an attribution line in front of them, and the woman who names the second cost stands at the middle of that bench**; in the first edition she had no place, the line in front of the first turn named a man, and the narration after them said *her*. **The sentence denying that the keeper's hand went near the shelf now names the second slate it was always about**, so that it and the later paragraph about the register can both stand. **A phrase carried over from an earlier volume with no referent in this one is cut**, and the sentence says there is no place in that room where a name like hers could be written down. **The middle column is now said to be full and claims no number of ruled lines.** No bolded word was touched, **the second cost is still named by somebody who is not the person it is about, the person it is about still has no name, no age, no number and no descriptor on any page, and a man still stands up in the middle of that room and does not ask and sits down again.**
- **0548** — **THE WALL.** The stone above the top edge of the board is now not the colour it was the morning before the water came, which is the state Chapter 0542 left it in, and the sentence no longer names a wash that Chapter 0542 says never happened before. **The volume's new question, the man of about fifty-nine at the window, the question put to him and not to the room, and the room not hearing it, all stand.**
- **0550** — **THREE THINGS, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S LAST IMAGE.** **The opening no longer repeats Chapter 0542's description of the morning wash word for word**; the board still comes off its two nails at the fourth hour, is washed, goes back on them with three sets of figures on it and none of the three rubbed out, and the washing still stops at the top of it. **The stone above that line is now not the colour it was the morning before the water came.** **The middle column of the book on the shelf is now said to be full and claims no number of ruled lines.** **The last image itself is untouched: a date in chalk on a door with no name under it, a board on two nails, a book on a shelf, a fence along the top of a road, and three barrows a hundred and fifty-nine days out measured from the day they went down it.**

**AND THE FIGURES FOR THE TEN AFTER THE REPAIR, on the definitions at the batch record §1.1 and §1.7, re-measured after the last edit and not before: the bolded share per chapter **12.23, 11.64, 13.37, 9.80, 13.23, 5.84, 12.14, 9.12, 9.85 and 8.05**, a range of 5.84 to 13.37 at an unweighted mean of 10.53 and a weighted mean of 10.54 on **11,606 words in 160 prose paragraphs** (first edition 11,518 in 158) with **1,223 words inside bold marks, which did not move**; the exchange **seventeen plain and eleven answered, unchanged**; the longest bolded turn **eighty-six words in Chapter 0547, unchanged**; paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker **zero in all ten, and zero on the harder reading that checks every marked turn has a plain speech or an attribution line in front of it**; the word-run check at forty words **zero across the ten files and zero inside each file, measured**, after three runs of forty-six, forty-two and forty-three words were cut; the sixteen-word threshold **two hundred and thirty-one shared with a file outside the batch and eighty-six shared inside it, or one hundred and fifty-four and seventy-five on the stricter reading, and the reading travels with the figure**; the elapsed figures **four in three files, each naming its anchor in its own sentence**; no unbalanced bold or quotation mark in any of the ten files.**

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED. The full record is `state/volume-11-close.md`, and `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0550.md` is the last chapter of Volume 11 and there is no Chapter 0551 in this repository.**

**Volume 11 runs Chapters 0501 to 0550 on days 501 to 550, one chapter to one day, and the entry above carries all fifty. The day map re-derived from day 1 being a Tuesday gives zero mismatches against the plan's own table and against `bible/power-system.md` §58 on chapter-equals-day, on the weekday and on the Bare-Month ordinal, and the pressure rotation agrees with the day map cell by cell at zero conflicts.**

**AND THE FOUR CHAPTERS OF THIS VOLUME THAT NO CLOSING-SLOT CHECK AND NO CROSS-FILE WORD-RUN CAN SEE, WHICH IS WHY THEY ARE NAMED HERE AND NOT ONLY IN THE CLOSE RECORD. Chapter 0503 is the day the first one-place form came out of a drawer and onto a shelf, and its figure is still in that one place and it is still in this city fifty days later. Chapter 0510 is the day both one-place forms stood on one table a full table's width apart with nothing laid against either. Chapter 0525 is the day the decision was taken and the cost was named out loud first, and it is the volume's nearer middle day. Chapter 0540 is the day the wall said one thing, and it is the only panel in the fifty files and the man of about thirty-one refused it in about four seconds and it cost him his own silence.**

**AND THE FIGURES FOR THE FIFTY, measured by this close on the fifty files and published with the reading and the file count in `state/volume-11-close.md` §7.2: the bolded share per file runs 3.41 to 21.17 at an unweighted mean of 10.82 and a weighted mean of 10.95 on 55,850 words in 743 prose paragraphs with 6,118 words inside bold marks; paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker zero in all fifty and the unrestricted reading returns 540, which is the narration count; paragraphs carrying a bold mark and no quotation mark zero; the exchange eighty-two plain and seventy-two answered at eighty-seven point eight per cent, and the ten that are not are broken by an attribution line in eight files and every one of the ten is named in the close; the longest bolded turn ninety-three words in Chapter 0539; the longest run of speech paragraphs three, in four files; and the four paragraph composition counts on Reading A are 203 speech, 216 one-sentence narration, 30 lead-ins, 186 free-standing and 154 paragraphs of three or more sentences, of which 115 carry a physical action and every one of the fifty files has at least one.**

**AND THE THREE FIGURES A WRITER INHERITING THIS VOLUME MUST NOT TAKE ON FAITH. The pool is 549 chapter files and not 499. The sixteen-word word-run figure is seventy-one runs shared with a file outside this volume and four hundred and seventy-three shared with another file inside it, and not zero, and the inside figure is the one no batch record of this volume measured. The descriptor map returns seventy-eight distinct descriptors on 549 files, which is the one published figure out of four that reproduces on a re-derivation, and the number resolver is the difference and not the corpus. AND THE WITHIN-FILE WORD-RUN PASS AT SIXTEEN WORDS RETURNS THREE RUNS IN THREE FILES AND NOT ZERO, AND THE SIXTEEN-WORD CROSS-FILE ROWS DID NOT REPRODUCE ON A RE-DERIVATION, so this close's own first-edition figures stand printed beside the second editions and a batch re-derives before it plans.**

# VOLUME 12, BATCH 0001

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED. The full record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0001.md`, and `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0551.md` through `chapter-0560.md` are on disk. `outline/ending.md` was not opened by the phase that wrote this block.**

**AND A REVIEW REPAIR PASS HAS CORRECTED TWO THINGS IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT CORRECTED THE RECORD AND NOT A CHAPTER. The Chapter 0554 entry gave the man of about thirty-four a bolded turn that is spoken in that chapter by an undescribed man at a window, and the figures paragraph certified a measurement that had not been re-run after the batch repaired its own five files. Both are corrected in place, both first editions are published beside them, and no chapter file was opened for editing and no prose moved.**

**Volume 12 runs Chapters 0551 to 0600 on days 551 to 600, one chapter to one day. This block carries the first ten, days 551 to 560. The run was re-derived in the batch's own run from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else, at zero mismatches against `outline/volume-12.md` §14.3 on chapter-equals-day, on the weekday and on the Bare-Month ordinal, and the Bare-Month day is the day less 315. No chapter of this ten uses an ordinal for a month, and the largest Bare-Month ordinal printed in these ten is the two hundred and forty-fifth.**

**Chapter 0551 — A Second Pass Before The Light Came Off The Flags.** Day 551, a Saturday, the two hundred and thirty-sixth day of the Bare Month. The outside wall at one end of that market, about the fourth hour and again about the seventh. **Adrian Vale's hands are on the board, the trough, the rag, the string and the second pass, and he wanted the second pass to be the last thing he does before the light comes off the flags, and he got it.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board has washed it every morning since the seventh week of this flood and says, in the bolded turn, that a board washed twice in one morning is still a board with three things on it and not two, and nobody in that row said he was right and nobody asked him a second question. The three sets of figures are on the page: the account of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood in an office's own hand that came down that road four hundred miles, and two figures of this city's own in chalk that do not agree with each other, and **not one of the three carries a line of words under it.** The wall above the top edge of that board is not washed. **He is the cause of a woman at a stall two along losing about a quarter of an hour of the front of that stall to water he put down that row at the seventh hour, and nobody in that row said a word to him about it.** Change: a board is washed twice in one morning and it is still a board with three things on it, and the second pass cost somebody else an hour.

**Chapter 0552 — A Sheet On A Table With One Place Already Filled.** Day 552, a Sunday, the two hundred and thirty-seventh. The room over a market, about the ninth hour. **A satchel with a flap comes up the road on the quarterly round and is set on a table under the one window and IS NOT OPENED, and the man who carried it does not ask what is in it and nobody in the room asks him.** **The sheet has a heading and ONE place ruled under it and the one place is already filled, with a figure of this city, in a hand that is not the hand of anybody in that room, and the figure is right.** The figure is printed nowhere in this chapter. The heading is a heading and not a sentence. **The chapter says in one plain sentence of narration that nobody in that room can check that figure and does not say why it thinks so.** About nine people look at it for four hours. The man of about thirty-one says in the bolded turn that he knows what is in it and did not open it on the road and is not going to open it in this room. Change: a figure of this city is on a table in a room and it is a figure of this city that this city did not make.

**Chapter 0553 — A Count Of Papers That Two Mouths Did Not Agree On.** Day 553, a Monday, the two hundred and thirty-eighth. The room over a market, the seventh hour to the ninth. **Six documents are named: three with four places ruled under a heading and three with a single place. Two sheets of one shape are in this room together this morning and NEITHER IS LAID BESIDE THE OTHER, NEITHER IS IN ONE HAND, AND NEITHER IS COMPARED OUT LOUD IN A MOUTH, and about four people in the room have noticed there are two of them and about four have not.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register counts them out loud and says a number, and a man near the step says a different one, and she answers him in the bolded turn and will not say her number a second time. **The count is wrong and the chapter does not correct it and does not say the count is wrong, and at the end of the chapter nobody in that room can say which of the two numbers was the one anybody should be counting.** The register's middle column holds five; the column on the right is empty from the top of the page to the bottom; the second slate stands on its edge with a day cut across the head of it and nothing under it and is not picked up, not turned over and not written on. Change: a room counted its own papers and got two answers.

**Chapter 0554 — A Number And Nothing On The Paper Saying What It Counts.** Day 554, a Tuesday, the two hundred and thirty-ninth. The room over a market, about the eighth hour. **A sheet is turned about on a table under a window and nobody's hand is on it while it is turned.** **AN UNDESCRIBED MAN AT THE WINDOW SAYS, IN THE BOLDED TURN, IN EIGHT WORDS, THAT NOBODY IN THAT ROOM CAN SAY WHAT THAT NUMBER COUNTS, and nobody improves on him and nobody thanks him and nobody asks him a second question. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL IS TWO STALLS ALONG IN THE ROW BELOW THAT WINDOW WITH HIS OWN HAND FLAT OVER HIS OWN INSIDE BREAST POCKET AND SAYS NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT ANY OF IT, and the first edition of this entry gave him that speech and it is not his.** A woman says somebody has to go and find out, and he says, in the second bolded turn, that he is not going and neither is anybody in that room and that there is nobody four hundred miles off who is going to be shown what it counted. **The volume's first discovery, and it is small and it is about a surface: there is nothing on that sheet, or beside it, or under it, that says what the figure is a figure of, and the sheet does not say and the heading is a heading and not a sentence.** The register-keeper does not put her hand flat on the wood, which is what she does. Change: the gap on the paper is named out loud by one man and nobody acts on it.

**Chapter 0555 — The Form Read Out With A Date In Chalk On The Door.** Day 555, a Wednesday, the two hundred and fortieth. The room over a market, about the ninth hour, the two hundred and fortieth day of the Bare Month in chalk on the door with no name under it. **The third one-place form is read out in the local language in daylight in front of about nine people who were already in the room, and the reader says what it is for out loud before anybody asks, and she says it twice and says it the same way both times.** The chapter says in one plain sentence of narration that nobody in that room had been told in advance what the morning was for, and that there has not been four days before anything in this city for five volumes running. **The form is to be answered with a figure of this city and to go back up the road on the quarterly round, and the figure already in the one place is not going to be rubbed out and nobody in the room is going to be told what it is a figure of. THE FIGURE IS NOT PRINTED.** She lifts the sheet off the wood and holds it up so the row at the back can see the one place in it, and puts it down the same way round. Change: a document has been read out and the room knows what it is for and nobody was told in advance.

**Chapter 0556 — A Wall And A Book And About Four People Who Knew It.** Day 556, a Thursday, the two hundred and forty-first. That market and the outside wall, about the seventh hour. **A woman with a stall says a number on that wall and a number in the room upstairs are the same kind of number, and the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is right about it in about four seconds: a wall of figures is read across and a book of figures is read down, the one came down here on a form with one place under a heading out of an office nine hundred miles off, the other two were put on in chalk in this city on two mornings, they are of one kind of thing and they are not the same thing, and nothing in four hundred miles is going to turn one of them round and stand it beside the other.** Nobody in that row said he was right, nobody improved on him, nobody asked him which of the three a person is meant to be reading. **About four people in that row had worked out that the two are of one kind of thing and about four had not, and not one of the two halves said one word to the other half.** A man near the gate says to write the same number on the wall, and the answer is that nobody in this city is going to put a figure on that wall. **The seven sheets that went out are named and there has not been an eighth one, and nobody wrote anything and nobody said a sheet was going out that week.** Change: a man in a row is right about two surfaces and nobody in the row says so.

**Chapter 0557 — A Lid That Shut And Nobody In That Market Noticed.** Day 557, a Friday, the two hundred and forty-second. That market, the outside wall and a step, about the fourth hour. The board is washed and goes back on its nails and the wall above it is not washed. **Adrian Vale's hands are on the lid of the box of chalk and on the grit in the flags in front of it, and he wanted that lid to shut, and it shut, and the only reason it shut is that he carried the grit off the flags in front of it and nobody asked him to and nobody noticed.** He is the cause of a woman with a stall two along getting about half an hour of her morning that she had not expected to have, and she says out loud that somebody has put the water off her front and that she does not know who and is not going to stand there and work it out, and **nobody in that row answered her.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall comes out from behind his own trestle to get a length of string off a nail, with his back to a man on the flags, and says in the bolded turn that the box has shut and that he has not touched it and neither has that man on the flags, and nobody in that row said he was right and nobody thanked him. **The two chalks are on the page and neither was used, and the full form of the day the second one went into that pocket is printed at full length and is not shortened.** Change: a lid shuts in a market and nobody in that market knows it.

**Chapter 0558 — Eleven Miles Of Flats And Nothing Taken Off Them.** Day 558, a Saturday, the two hundred and forty-third. Eleven miles of flats with a channel of wet silt, the fifth hour to the eighth, in weather. **A trough, a bucket and a rag go out along eleven miles of flats and come back up the same road, and NOBODY COUNTS ANYTHING, and there is no fifth morning of the count in this volume and this chapter is not a morning of it.** The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is on that ground with his board under his arm and does not trade and says one thing about the wind that nobody acts on. **A person is on that ground with a slate under an arm and a tin in two hands, and she is given no name, no age, no number and no descriptor, nobody on that ground spoke to her, nobody asked her anything, and nobody thanked her, and she is in no room.** The four mornings of the count on foot are named as the five hundred and eleventh, the five hundred and fifteenth, the five hundred and seventeenth and the five hundred and twenty-first days of this flood, and the day each was made is named in the same chapter, and **there is no fifth.** Change: a body walks and carries and nothing comes out of it.

**Chapter 0559 — A Chair Out From Under A Shelf And Put Back.** Day 559, a Sunday, the two hundred and forty-fourth. The room over a market, the back of it, about the sixth hour. **A chair is pulled out from under the shelf behind the bench, a shelf is looked at from below, and NOTHING ON THE SHELF IS TOUCHED.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall comes up that stair and says, in the bolded turn, that there is a thing on the bottom of that shelf nobody has looked at in a year and that he is not going to say what the thing is, and nobody in that room said he was right and nobody thanked him. **He goes down on one knee and looks along the underside of the shelf and the underside has two brackets and a nail on it and nothing else, and he does not reach up and does not put anything under that shelf and does not take anything off it.** The register was not opened, its middle column still holds five, the column on the right runs the whole height of the page with nothing in it, and he does not put a hand on the book and does not say one word about that column. **The second slate is not picked up, not turned over and not written on, and no hand in that room but the keeper's went near that shelf and hers did not go near it.** Change: one thing changes in that room and it is a rectangle of floor that will be gone in about a week.

**Chapter 0560 — Three Sheets Counted And Two Numbers About A Room.** Day 560, a Monday, the two hundred and forty-fifth. The room over a market, about the ninth hour. **Three sheets of three different shapes are taken off two places and set on one table with a length of the wood between each and the next, and NONE OF THE THREE IS LAID BESIDE ANOTHER, none is ever in one hand with another, and none is compared out loud in a mouth.** Six documents are named and the sheet that went out of this city fifteen days ago, measured from the day it went out, is named as not in this city and not coming back. A man at the far end of that bench says the count is three out of four papers, and the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is right about it in about four seconds: a register is a book and a book is not a form and it does not go up that road. Nobody said he was right. **The two chalks are on the page with the full form of the day the second one went into that pocket printed at full length, the store's chalk was never used, and the length of new rope over the back of a chair is named once, is unused, is lifted by no hand, and nobody in that room looked at it once.** The three sheets go back to the two places they came off in the order they came off in, the second slate does not move. Change: the batch ends with two numbers about a room and neither of them a figure of this city.

**AND THE FIGURES FOR THE TEN, on the definitions published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0001.md` and RE-MEASURED IN A REVIEW REPAIR PASS AGAINST THE FILES AS THEY NOW STAND, AFTER THE LAST EDIT AND NOT BEFORE — the first edition of this paragraph said "re-measured after the last edit and not before" and did not do it, and the numbers it carried were the pre-repair ones, and both faults are named here rather than corrected quietly: the bolded share per file runs 4.77 to 17.42 at an unweighted mean of 7.58 and a weighted mean of 7.55 on 10,769 words with 813 words inside bold marks; paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker zero in all ten on the first-person-restricted reading, and 93 on the unrestricted reading, which is the count of body paragraphs carrying no quotation mark over 119 body paragraphs; paragraphs carrying a bold mark and no quotation mark zero; the exchange thirteen plain and seven answered, and the six that are not answered are on the six pages where nobody is waiting for anybody to be useful; the longest bolded turn ninety-two words in Chapter 0556, at the working ceiling and under the hard ceiling, and it was not cut; the longest run of speech paragraphs two, in six files; the four paragraph composition counts on Reading A are 26 speech, 31 one-sentence narration, 3 lead-ins, 28 free-standing and 34 paragraphs of three or more sentences, and every one of the ten files has at least one paragraph of three or more sentences carrying a physical action on a reading and not a sweep, and the swept figure the first edition published is withdrawn because the population it was taken over no longer exists and its keyword list was never published; the word-run check at forty words zero across the ten files and zero inside each file, measured, after five duplications at that threshold were found and repaired; at sixteen words 233 runs shared with a file outside the batch and 2 inside a single file, longest thirty-nine, and every run is published with the file it stands in and named as a required declaration and none is repaired; the elapsed figures three in three files, each naming its anchor in its own sentence; and the two hundred and thirty-sixth to the two hundred and forty-fifth are the only Bare-Month days these ten print.**

**AND TWO FIGURES THE NEXT BATCH MUST NOT TAKE ON FAITH, PUBLISHED BESIDE WHAT THEY DISAGREE WITH AND RESOLVED BY NOBODY: the plan's own §9 item 50 and §11 print the three who went down the road as one hundred and sixty-nine days out at day 600, and its own §20.7 prints them as two hundred and nine at day 600 measured from day 391, and 600 less 391 is 209 and 169 is the value for day 560, and this batch printed neither and repaired neither. And the day on the door, which the plan's §20.7 gives to every chapter of the fifty, is on the page in six of these ten and not in the four that are market and ground chapters.**

**AND THE THREE FIGURES A WRITER OF DAYS 561 TO 570 MUST NOT TAKE ON FAITH. The pool is 559 chapter files at the hour it was measured and was 549 before this batch's ten existed, and both are printed wherever a figure is printed. The forty-word word-run figure is zero on both passes and it was not zero on the first pass. The descriptor instrument returns 101 distinct descriptors against a published 78 and its per-volume row agrees at three of eleven cells, and one published whole-manuscript cell comes back lower on a re-run, and all three are published beside what they disagree with and none is corrected. AND THE PLAN IS OF TWO MINDS ABOUT THE PERSON WITH A SLATE AND A TIN, one page of her in this volume or none, and the disagreement is published and resolved by nobody.**

# VOLUME 12, BATCH 0002

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED. The full record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0002.md`, and `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0561.md` through `chapter-0570.md` are on disk. `outline/ending.md` was not opened by the phase that wrote this block, and this block was not written by that phase — see the repair note at the foot of it.**

**THE ENTRY FOR DAYS 561 TO 570 WAS OMITTED BY THE BATCH AND IS WRITTEN HERE, AND THE OMISSION IS PUBLISHED RATHER THAN QUIETLY CLOSED. `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` line 141 put `state/chapter-summaries.md` outside the writable set, so the batch wrote no block here, and `AGENTS.md` line 52 requires a chapter summary from every batch regardless. The next batch was left with nothing here for the ten chapters it continues from. This block is that entry and it is written from the ten files as they stand, not from the batch's account of them. The batch's own claim that its prompt did not list this file was true and is not a defence.**

Volume 12 runs Chapters 0551 to 0600 on days 551 to 600, one chapter to one day. This block carries days 561 to 570. The run was re-derived from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else, at zero mismatches against `outline/volume-12.md` §14.3 on chapter-equals-day, on the weekday and on the Bare-Month ordinal, and the Bare-Month day is the day less 315. No chapter of these ten uses an ordinal for a month, and the largest Bare-Month ordinal printed in them is the two hundred and fifty-fifth.

**Chapter 0561 — A Plank That Held Two Thirds Of The Way.** Day 561, a Tuesday, the two hundred and forty-sixth day of the Bare Month. Eleven miles of flats in weather, from about the seventh hour to about the ninth. **Adrian Vale's hands are on the plank and on the wet sill at one end of the second row, and he wanted the thing to stay down where he put it, and it did not go far enough.** He lays a plank from the sill to the stone across a gap; it holds about two thirds of the way and stops. A man in a coat open at the front with a bundle under his arm puts his foot on it, the near end comes off the sill, the plank turns about the width of a hand at the far end and the whole of it goes over, and the man goes into the gap up to his chest and comes out on his hands and knees with the bundle under his arm the whole time. A woman waiting at the far end of the same sill turns about and goes back up her own way. **Nobody on that ground asked who put the plank down, nobody thanked him and nobody said he was right.** The four mornings of the count on foot are named by the day each was made, and there is no fifth. Change: a body lays a thing on a wet stone and the thing does not go far enough, and two people go back the way they came.

**Chapter 0562 — Sixteen Posts And Eleven Withies And Nobody Counting Them.** Day 562, a Wednesday, the two hundred and forty-seventh. Out past the last named house, the top of a shut road, from about the sixth hour. **The man of about sixty, with a hazel mallet and a coil of rope, put that fence up and says so out loud in about nine words, and is not asked why, and says he does not know what the corner is for and will not pretend otherwise.** The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and the same cord through all eleven, and the corner at the far end runs about the width of a hand and stops. A person with a tin pail comes up the low side of that road four times, cannot get past the corner, and comes back each time with the pail about half full, and speaks to nobody. Nobody counted the posts, nobody took a measure, no post was moved, no withy was bound. Change: a man says he built a thing and will not say what it is for, and a corner is left open on a road four people walk up and down.

**Chapter 0563 — One Ruled Place And Another Ruled Place.** Day 563, a Thursday, the two hundred and forty-eighth. The room over a market, about the eighth hour. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register puts a hand flat on the table, then on the spine of the book, then takes both off, and says nothing.** The sheet on the table has a heading and one place ruled under it and the one place is already filled with a figure of this city in a hand that is not the hand of anybody in that room, and the figure is right. **The volume's second discovery, and it is about a shape and not about a number: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says that a single ruled place under a heading and a single ruled place in a column are the same thing ruled twice on two sheets of paper, and that this room has had both of them for a month and has not said so.** A woman near the step says put one in the other; the keeper answers that nobody in this city is going to put a figure of its own into somebody else's place on a form that has to go back up that road. **Nobody said he was right, nobody improved on him, nobody thanked him and nobody asked him a second question.** The near edges of the sheet and the open page stand about a hand's width apart with nothing in the gap. Change: a room is told out loud that it has been holding the same thing twice and does nothing about it.

**Chapter 0564 — Nineteen One Way And A Different Number The Other.** Day 564, a Friday, the two hundred and forty-ninth. That market, a wall at the end of a row, from about the seventh hour. **The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board sets his board down and counts it across and gets nineteen, turns it on the ground with the toe of his boot so the rows run away from him instead of across, and gets a different number.** He says in the bolded turn that it is nineteen down the rows and not nineteen down the columns, that he has known that for nine years and has never said it to anybody, and that there is nothing on the board to stop a reader getting it off it himself. **He says neither number out loud as a count of the board and nobody in that row asks him which of the two is the count.** A woman with a stall two along gets the same different number and does not know he got it. A fourth person reads the board from the far end with his head the other way up. **There is a space at the foot of the middle column about as wide as two fingers, there was a name in it in a hand of his own, and he took it off himself about nine years ago and has not said so to anybody.** Change: a man says out loud that a board gives two numbers and nobody in the row says which one the board has.

**Chapter 0565 — Four Hundred Yards Of Flags And Nobody Counting Them.** Day 565, a Saturday, the two hundred and fiftieth. That market, the flags, from about the fourth hour. **Adrian Vale has the trough, the bucket in it and the rag still on the stone lip of the step, and he wanted the water off that row, and he put a trough across a gutter and made it worse.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board has washed two hundred yards of a four hundred yard row every morning for a year and nobody in that row has ever said so to him. Adrian works from the channel end, does nine bucketfuls, does not put the bucket over the lip, and does not use the other man's trough and is not asked to. **He is on the flags before the man who washes the board, and at about the eighth hour he carries the full trough to the near gate and sets it across the gutter, and the water comes along the row behind it and stands in a flat sheet about as deep as a thumb across the front of three stalls, and a man carries two crates and a basket off the flags onto his own trestle and is wet to the knee doing it.** He does not know why the water was there and nobody in that row told him. The wall above the top edge of that board was not washed. **The trough was never over its lip on any of these ten days.** Change: a man clears a row of standing water by damming the gutter that drains it, and a stranger pays for it.

**Chapter 0566 — The Corner Nobody Has Taken A Cord To.** Day 566, a Sunday, the two hundred and fifty-first. The top of that shut road, the corner of that fence, from about the seventh hour. **The man of about fifty-seven stands at the corner and nobody in this city has asked that man anything at any hour of this flood, and nobody asks him that morning, and he does not say where the fence was built or by whom or when.** A man comes along the top of that road with a short length of cord with a loop tied in one end of it, stands at the corner, looks at the two posts and at the slack in the withy nearest the shut end, **does not put the cord round, does not use it and does not leave it, and goes back the way he came.** The woman at the near end of the fence says that corner is the one unfinished place on that road; the answer is that nobody is going to get round it on foot before the middle of the week, and that he did not come out to see to it and did not come out to be told about it. **Nobody said he was right, nobody improved on him, nobody thanked him, and he is not going to be asked anything about that fence in this city by anybody.** **Whether that fence stands between the right two things is a question nobody on that ground asked and nobody answered, and nobody took a measure of that fence in order to ask it.** Change: the same corner, four days on, is still open and a man with exactly the right cord in his hands walks away from it.

**Chapter 0567 — Four And Three And Nobody Told Which One To Count.** Day 567, a Monday, the two hundred and fifty-second. The room over a market, about the ninth hour. **About nine people, none of them sent for, put three sheets out on one table and a fourth went out of the room's count before anybody started.** A man at the step says that is three of them and there were four of them. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says four have come up that road and one has not been in this room and is not going to be counted this morning, and that a person can count what is in front of that person and get three and be right about what is in front of that person. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register says the other number out loud herself, and not one of the nine says which of the two was the one anybody should be counting.** **The seven sheets that have gone out of this city in this flood are named, every one written by somebody in this city, and this city has not written one since, and this volume writes none.** Change: a room counts what has come up its road in this flood and gets two answers and keeps both.

**Chapter 0568 — A Figure That Came Down The Road And A Figure That Went Up It.** Day 568, a Tuesday, the two hundred and fifty-third. The room over a market, about the seventh hour to the eleventh. **The volume's third discovery, and it is about kind rather than about shape: the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board says in about four seconds that one figure came up that road and one figure went up it, that they are the same kind of number, and that there is nothing in this city that will put the two of them in one order.** A man near the step says take the one that came down and write it up on that board and the two will agree; the answer is that nobody is writing a figure of this city up on that wall, that the wall has three on it that do not agree, and that adding a fourth would not make the other three agree and would make four. **Nobody said he was right, nobody improved on him, nobody thanked him, nobody asked him a second question, and the register-keeper was in that room and was not asked which of the two she thought a person ought to be using.** The register's right-hand column is empty the whole height of the page and she did not look at it; the second slate was not lifted, not turned and not written on. **Neither of the two figures is written out on any page in four hundred miles, no page puts them as two figures, no page puts them as one figure, and no page says where either of them came from.** Change: a man says two numbers are the same kind of number, and a room hears it and puts nothing in an order.

**Chapter 0569 — A Hurdle He Made And A Bar He Replaced.** Day 569, a Wednesday, the two hundred and fifty-fourth. The room over a market, about the tenth hour. **The man of about fifty-nine carries a hurdle he made up nine steps' worth of stair and stands it against a wall, and there is a bar in it that is not the bar that was in it.** The new bar is about a head's width off the width of the old one and is set a finger's width higher on the left-hand side than on the right, and the two marks the old bar left are still in the wood on both uprights and nothing has been planed out of them. He says in the bolded turn that it stands up out of the way against a wall, that that is what it is for, and that he put the bar in himself and is not going to stand there and talk about a bar. **Nobody in that room asked him about the three who went down that road, and he did not offer it, and he has been asked once before in this city and did not answer it then and is not going to be asked it again in this volume.** A party of four is four and three of them went down the four hundred miles with a barrow apiece on the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood. Change: a man replaces a bar in his own fence and the room does not ask him the one question it has been saving.

**Chapter 0570 — A Room Working Out What A Thing On A Table Is For.** Day 570, a Thursday, the two hundred and fifty-fifth. The room over a market, about the ninth hour. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register says once, and does not say it again that morning, that it is a form with a heading on it and one place ruled under that heading, that the one place holds a figure of this city that this city did not put there, that the form is to be answered with a figure of this city and to go back up that road, and that nobody in this room is going to be told what that figure counts.** **The four days that would have been the cooling are not available in this city and cannot be arranged on a morning, and this is the fifth volume running that has found out what it is being asked at a table on the morning it is asked.** A man near the step says there is no place on it for the day and none for the week either; the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living answers that the round goes up that road four times in the year and goes up with whoever is going up that road that week, that nobody in this city has written down which week that is, and that he knows what is in that satchel and did not open it on the road and is not going to open it in this room. **There is no satchel in that room and there has not been one since the morning it came up that stair, and not one surface in this city carries a line of words under a figure, and nothing was written under anything at any hour of that morning, and the one place was not filled.** Change: a room works out what a form is for and decides nothing, and the figure that was already in the one place stays there.

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE ACROSS THESE TEN DAYS, WHICH IS THE LIST A NEXT BATCH NEEDS. No day, no weekday, no Bare-Month ordinal, no anchor, no form, no notice, no object count that holds, no cast member and no decision of record moved. The register's right-hand column is empty from the top of the page to the bottom on all ten of these days and nothing was put in it. The second slate is on its shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under it, and was not picked up, not turned over and not written on, and no hand but the keeper's went near that shelf on any of the ten. The wall said nothing on all ten of these days and there is no panel in these ten chapters. No sheet went anywhere on any of these ten days. Adrian Vale is in two of the ten and is Stage 2 on both, performs no working and opens no threshold. No new final enemy, no new cosmic layer, no new antagonist and no new world was introduced. The eleven inherited debts and the six named at `outline/volume-12.md` §21 are carried forward unpaid and no chapter of this batch repaired one, took one or rerouted one.**

**AND THE TWO THINGS A WRITER OF DAYS 571 TO 580 MUST KNOW ABOUT THIS BLOCK, BOTH OF THEM THE PRODUCT OF THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS BELOW. First, the closing paragraph of the BATCH 0001 block above is a set of warnings addressed to "a writer of days 561 to 570"; that batch has now been written, so those warnings are spent, and they are not carried forward as a live briefing for the next ten days. Second, every figure in the BATCH 0001 block was measured on the files as they stood before the repair pass below and none of them was re-measured afterwards; re-derive before you plan.**

**AND THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS ON THIS BATCH, WHICH MOVED PROSE AND NOTHING ELSE. Seven chapters of the ten were opened for editing and three were not, and no story fact, no day, no count, no descriptor, no cast member and no outcome moved in any of the seven. The repairs were: the verbatim reuse of sentences already on the page in earlier chapters of this volume, seven in all, removed by rewording while the motif each one carries was kept — the chalk date on the door with no name under it is still chalked on that door in 0562, 0563, 0567, 0569 and 0570 and is worded differently in each; the census of about four people who were in and about four who were not is still the volume's census; and "a woman near the step said the thing that is said" is still the volume's attribution tag. A garbled sentence in 0565 that read "the walk is the walk and the four hundred yards is a walk and not a figure about a place" was rewritten to say that nobody has ever measured that four hundred yards or written it down, which is the chapter's own claim and was always its meaning. The filler measure "about as long as it takes a man to walk from one place to another and back" stood four times in 0561, three in 0565 and twice in each of 0563 and 0564, and now stands at most twice in any one chapter, in two different forms. The possessive "his own" ran at one in every forty-eight words in 0564 and one in every seventy-six in 0565 and now runs at one in every three hundred and four and one in every one hundred and thirty-one. All ten chapters remain between eight hundred and thirty-three and one thousand one hundred and seventy-eight words, and the cross-file word-run check at forty words inside Volume 12 returns zero runs on both passes, which is the figure the batch itself published and the figure this pass preserved.**
