**THE TEN DAYS, AND THE STORY COMES FIRST.** A room found out that about eight people a morning go past a door at the foot of a stair, that about four of them read the date on it and about four of them do not, and that the wood under that date has been bare for sixty-eight days; the keeper of the register said out loud, in her own mouth, that she would answer what may be written there if she could and cannot, and she is thanked by nobody, and the man who keeps a stall two stalls along stood in that doorway for the first morning since the water came and came back in and said one thing that was not about the wood. **A trough and a bucket walked off the end of a market row between the fourth hour and the ninth hour and nobody in that row said where they went and nobody asked the man who washed the board, because he did not know either.** A room of about nine people was asked in ordinary conversation, across the middle of the floor, what may be written on that door, and about four of them said they did not know and about four said nothing at all and the woman who keeps the book was not asked. **Out along eleven miles of flats a length of cord would not come off a gate-peg it had been on the day before, and a man carried a trough and a bucket four hundred yards of flags and set them down in the middle of a row he works in, and the man who washes that board put his own hand into that bucket at the ninth hour and found washed stone off the face of a board in the bottom of it, and said so out loud, and nobody in that row said he was right.** A lid on a box of chalk did not shut and did not shut on any day of this volume, and the grit under the near side of that rim was not counted, and a board came off two nails and was washed once and went back up on the same two nails. Two men at one end of one market, nine years, and neither of them had ever asked the other one thing. Four people in a room meant four different things about a strip of wood about as wide as two of a man's fingers and not one of the four of them was wrong. A plank lay across a wet sill and a man went over the top of it and came back and was never told whose hands laid it there. **Seven notices went out of this city in this flood and there is no eighth and no notice was written on that morning, so a room found out what it was being asked on the morning it was asked, and a man named the cost of carrying a thing up that stair out loud first, and nobody thanked him for it, and the man it fell on was in the room and was not thanked either.** A woman lifted a chair out from under a shelf in the corner of that room and nothing on that shelf was touched at any hour of the morning, and she did not know why she had moved it, and a man came up that stair, said one thing he was not going to be told the answer to, and went back down it.

- **0651, day 651, a Monday, the three hundred and thirty-sixth day of the Bare Month.** The first discovery, and it is about a surface. About eight people go past that door in the ordinary course of a morning, about four of them read the date and about four of them do not, and **not one of the eight looked at the wood under it.** The keeper of the register says out loud that she would answer what may be written there if she could and that she cannot, and the man who keeps a stall says he has gone past that door every morning of this flood and never once had a reason to stop at it, and then he stops at it, for the first morning since the water came, and comes back in and says one thing that is not about the wood. **The trough and the bucket that were standing against the foot of that wall at the fourth hour are gone at the ninth hour and nobody in that row says where they went and the man who washed the board does not know either.** The one sheet that ever carried a line of words set under a figure went out of this city early in this flood and is not coming back. Change: a surface nobody has asked about is named in front of a room, and nothing is done about it.
- **0652, day 652, a Tuesday, the three hundred and thirty-seventh.** A satchel with a flap stands behind a bench and the flap does not go up. A woman with a hand-basket says out loud that a room of nine people is the wrong nine to speak for a word that goes on a sheet four hundred miles off, and **the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board says out loud, twice, that he has never asked the keeper what is under the date on that door and that there is a difference between a thing he has not asked and a thing nobody has asked him.** Nineteen rates in four markets for nine years, and one name that is not on his board, and nobody in that room asked him about either. The book came off its shelf and went back on it and nothing went into the column on the right of that page. Change: a room is told it cannot speak, by a woman with a basket, and a man distinguishes the two kinds of not-asking.
- **0653, day 653, a Wednesday, the three hundred and thirty-eighth. ADRIAN VALE IS IN IT.** Eleven miles of flats in weather. He wants a length of cord off a gate-peg to come away and coil on the step where a person could find it, **and it does not come off, and it was on that peg at the fourth hour of this day and it was on that peg at the fourth hour of the day before.** He carries a trough and a bucket four hundred yards of flags and sets them down on the high ground in the middle of a row another man works in, and leaves the rag on the stone lip of the step where it lives, and a man he has never spoken to finds a trough standing there that is not his and says so. At the ninth hour the man who washes that board puts his own hand into that bucket and finds washed stone off the face of a board in the bottom of it, and says out loud that it is not water that came out of eleven miles of flats, and nobody in that row says he is right. Change: a man carries a thing to where it will be found, and the thing that is found in it was never in the water he thought.
- **0654, day 654, a Thursday, the three hundred and thirty-ninth.** Two men at one end of one market for nine years and neither of them has ever asked the other anything. **The man of about thirty-four's own piece of chalk went into that inside breast pocket on the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood and came out of it once, on the six hundred and thirty-eighth, and went back the same afternoon without having been used, and it is in that pocket and is not out of it.** The man of about thirty-nine says out loud, twice, that he is not going to ask him what the chalk is for and that the not-asking is nine years of not-asking and not a favour. A woman wants that board turned round so a price can be read off it and is told nothing. Change: two men name a thing they have both declined to ask about, in public, and neither of them asks.
- **0655, day 655, a Friday, the three hundred and fortieth. ADRIAN VALE IS IN IT.** A hand on the rim of the store's box of chalk, and a thumb's width of grit under the near side of that rim, and nobody counting it. **He wants the lid to shut and it does not shut on this day and it does not shut on any day of this volume.** A board comes off two nails and is washed once and goes back up on the same two nails, and **the wall above the top edge of that board is washed by no second person on any day of this volume and is not washed this morning.** The man of about thirty-four says out loud, twice, that he has never asked her about the column and never will, and the man of about thirty-seven is not in this book at any hour of this volume. Change: a lid that does not shut, a board washed once instead of twice, and two men saying out loud that they have never asked anybody anything.
- **0656, day 656, a Saturday, the three hundred and forty-first.** The second discovery, and it is a person and not a surface. **Nobody in this city can be shown one person who was ever told what may be written on that door.** A slate is turned over onto its face and put down and nothing is written on it and nothing is ruled on it and it is not carried back to the shelf. About nine people are asked in ordinary conversation, across the middle of that room, and about four say they do not know and about four say nothing at all, and the woman who keeps the book is not asked at any hour of the morning and says so herself, out loud, and is thanked by nobody. The man of about thirty-four finds out that he has been saying for years that the door belongs to that room and cannot point at one person who ever told him so, and says he is not going to say it in that room again. Change: the volume's central fact is established, that not one person can be named who was ever told, and the room works out that this is a different sort of thing from a thing that is forbidden.
- **0657, day 657, a Sunday, the three hundred and forty-second.** Four people in a room mean four different things about a strip of wood about as wide as two of a man's fingers: a name, nothing at all, a figure, and a thing left alone on purpose. **Not one of the four of them is wrong, and not one of the four of them is going to hear the other three say so.** The man of about thirty-four says out loud, twice, that a name in a strip is a thing a person can be asked about at a front on a wet morning and that he has not asked in nine years and is not going to start on a Sunday, and the man of about thirty-nine says out loud that not one of the four of them has asked him what is not in that strip and that is why he has never put anything in it. **The one name that is not on that board was taken off it in his own hand and has never been said to anybody.** Change: four people are each right, and the space on the board is not joined by anybody to the door or to the register, including the narrator.
- **0658, day 658, a Monday, the three hundred and forty-third. ADRIAN VALE IS IN IT.** Eleven miles of flats, a wet sill, and a plank. **He lays it there at the fourth hour and it is there at the seventh hour and at the tenth, and a man goes over the top of it and comes back, and nobody on that row ever tells him whose hands put it there.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board puts his own weight on it, pushes the near end a finger's width towards himself, lets it go and watches it settle back, and says out loud into an empty hut forty feet from that man that a plank across a wet sill is not a step and does not become one because a person has gone over the top of it twice, and the man it was said about forty feet away doing something else hears not one word of it. Change: a thing is laid down and used and its age is never established, and a thing said in a room is said into an empty room.
- **0659, day 659, a Tuesday, the three hundred and forty-fourth.** **Seven notices have gone out of this city in this flood and there is no eighth and none was written on that morning, and there is no notice for this morning and there is not going to be one.** The four days a notice gives a room is the cooling, and there is no cooling on this morning, and a room with nothing in front of it finds out what it is for on the morning it is being asked and not the morning before. **The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living names the cost out loud first and twice: he will carry one more thing up that stair and never know, and he would rather be a man who carried it and never knew than a man who asked at the bottom of that stair, and he is not going to be thanked for it because he has not told anybody what it was.** A man at the step wanted the room to say out loud what it was being asked for and nobody said it, and he went down that stair with his hand on the rail the whole way and was still not knowing at the bottom of it. The flap did not go up. Change: a cost is named first, in the decider's own mouth, and the person it falls on is in the room and is not thanked either.
- **0660, day 660, a Wednesday, the three hundred and forty-fifth, the last of this batch.** A chair comes out from under the shelf in the corner of that room at the sixth hour, a pail comes out from under the chair, and **nothing on that shelf is touched at any hour of that morning and no hand but the keeper's comes near it and that hand does not come onto it.** The shelf holds the register with five figures in one column and no sixth, and the second slate with a day across the head of it and nothing under it, and **it holds nothing else: the first of the four one-place sheets is in this city and is not on that shelf, and nobody in that room said where it is.** Seven documents have come up that road or gone out of it in this flood, three of four places and four of one place, and **that is the number of documents and not the number of notices, and a notice is not a document.** The fence past the last named house is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and is not measured and no post is moved, and a length of new rope lies over the back of a chair and no hand lifts it, cuts it or takes it up. The keeper says out loud that she moved the chair because the light does not come across that corner before about the tenth hour and she has never once seen what is on the bottom of it, and **the man of about thirty-four says out loud that he is going down that stair without having asked her one single thing about any of it.** Change: the one thing that moved in that room all morning is furniture, it moved twice, it went back the wrong way round, and the room has one thing less it can show somebody the beginning of, and the keeper does not know which one.

**AND WHAT THE TEN DAYS DID AND DID NOT DO.** Two costs were named out loud in front of about nine people and neither was thanked for, and one of them was named twice. The volume's central fact was established out loud in a room: **not one person in this city can be shown one other person who was ever told what may be written on the door at the foot of that stair, and a thing nine people have each been certain about for years and that not one of the nine can account for is a different sort of thing from a thing that is forbidden.** A trough and a bucket walked off a row and the man who moved them did not know where they went and was not asked. A rag went from a man's shoulder to a stone lip and lay on that lip until the light came off the flags. A lid did not shut. A board was washed once and went back up on the same two nails, and the wall above its top edge was washed by no second person. **Adrian Vale is in three of these ten, 0653, 0655 and 0658, which are the three the plan's own table names, and he performs no working, opens no threshold, ages nowhere, and is thanked by nobody and told by nobody that he was right, and the three outcomes are a want not obtained, a want not obtained and a want obtained.** The first of the four one-place sheets is named and is in this city and is not laid beside any of the other three and is not compared out loud and is not read out. A length of new rope is named and no hand lifts it. There is no eighth notice and no notice was written. **The door carries a date in chalk and nothing whatever stands under it on all ten days, and the wood below it is bare for the width of a hand and is not widened, not narrowed, not cleaned, not painted and not covered.** Nothing arrived, nothing was read out, nothing's one place was written in, nothing was shut and nothing went back up the road on any of these ten days. No new person, no new descriptor, no new name and no new place was introduced, and **these ten files carry zero `>` blocks and zero System panels, which is what `NOVEL_SPEC.md` requires and not a shortage.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-14/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, WRITING CHAPTERS 0661 TO 0670, DAYS 661 TO 670.** Day 661, 0665, 0671 and 0679 are Adrian Vale's days under `outline/volume-14.md` §4.1 and a card may not restate the wants in that table. **The next batch's card file does not exist and is not to be written by that batch as a plan; a card block written after the ten chapters are on disk is a reconstruction.**

## DAYS 661 TO 670 — CHAPTERS 0661 TO 0670 (BATCH 0002, WRITTEN TO THIS FILE BY THE BATCH THAT WROTE THE CHAPTERS)

**Note on writable set, published rather than resolved silently.** The prompt for Batch 0001 left this file out of its writable set and a review named the omission as a required deliverable; the same instruction conflict was named against Volume 13 Batch 0005 before it. This batch writes its block here anyway, at the foot, per `AGENTS.md`, and publishes the conflict: if a future prompt leaves this file out again, the block is still written and the conflict still published.

- **0661, day 661, Thursday, the 346th.** Wharf yard, grit kept, stack set right, barrow caught. Adrian tries the grit and fails; his pressing shifts the stack and rolls a barrow the wharf man must catch. Change: a path cleared and a board levelled by two pairs of hands, belonging to neither.
- **0662, day 662, Friday, the 347th.** The store's board comes down, is washed, goes back up; the wall stays out of it. A room puts board, wall and room side by side and the three disagree. Change: one clean face, one same silence.
- **0663, day 663, Saturday, the 348th.** Third discovery: a written page and an always-like page are the same page, said out loud; four people mean four things by writing and no two are wrong. Change: the room says what it can and the page keeps what it keeps.
- **0664, day 664, Sunday, the 349th.** The carrier shows a satchel shut and puts it back shut; the flap never rises. Change: the shut shown without the past shown.
- **0665, day 665, Monday, the 350th. ADRIAN VALE IS IN IT.** A board carried the yard's length goes onto the wrong stack, where no instrument can tell it. Change: a board dry at the cost of misplaced.
- **0666, day 666, Tuesday, the 351st.** Knife wiped uncut; two men name carrying as their cost and ask nothing of each other. Change: two costs named, neither thanked.
- **0667, day 667, Wednesday, the 352nd, the only recovery day of the ten.** Board down and up, nothing decided, wall silent, rag back on the lip. Change: one wet face made clean and called nothing.
- **0668, day 668, Thursday, the 353rd.** A question goes round four times about which surface to go by, answered four times with not-knowing, never put to the keeper. Change: a room that learns it cannot answer.
- **0669, day 669, Friday, the 354th.** Fourth discovery: the room-and-date-and-reading instrument cannot be used on a page somebody in it wrote in. The keeper alone to the tenth hour. Change: keeping held apart from saying.
- **0670, day 670, Saturday, the 355th.** A stall keeps its face in and its back bare while eight go past uncounted; the chalk never leaves the pocket. Change: a face hid and a back bare, thanked by nobody.

**AND WHAT THE TEN DAYS DID AND DID NOT DO.** Two Adrian days, both wants not obtained, both physical causes and no words. No round figure printed on any trap day; no same-figure pair joined; no elapsed road figure; no counting since any refusal. Nothing under the date on any day; no second word in the column; the chalk never out; the second slate never touched; the wall silent on all ten; zero panels. The two sevens named as two. No new person, no descriptor, no name, no place. The two frame repeats are Frame 1 on 0661/0670 and Frame 2 on 0662/0669, and no chapter has become the other.

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-14/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, WRITING CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680, DAYS 671 TO 680.** Days 0671, 0679 and 0680 are Adrian Vale's days under `outline/volume-14.md` §4.1 and a card may not restate the wants in that table. **No card file for that batch exists under `outline/` and none is to be written; its cards go at the head of its own block in `state/current.md` before Chapter 0671 exists.**

**This block restates the ten entries above as they stand after a review repair, and it exists because the entries above now describe files that no longer read the way the entries describe them. - **0665, day 665, Monday, the 350th.** A board goes onto the wrong stack. **Unchanged in event. The wharf man and the stallholder are now described in the tense of the scene, and Adrian's closing now ends on the board still leaning where he put it, where it will stay until somebody lifts it.**
- **0666, day 666, Tuesday, the 351st.** Knife wiped uncut; two men name carrying as their cost. **Unchanged in event, and this is the thinnest chapter of the ten at 724 words before the repair. Both men still ask the other nothing and neither is thanked.**

## VOLUME 14, BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 0671 TO 0680, DAYS 671 TO 680, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY. WRITTEN BY THE BATCH, APPENDED AT THE FOOT, AND NOTHING ABOVE IS EDITED.

**THE INSTRUCTION CONFLICT ON THIS BLOCK, PUBLISHED RATHER THAN RESOLVED, because the prompt for this batch says in §8 item 3 that this file is to be written by the batch and says in the same item that a later prompt may leave it out, and the block behind this one at the foot of this file says the same thing about itself.** The conflict is therefore between two instructions in the same paragraph of the same prompt, and it is resolved in favour of writing it, and the writing of it is what this block is, and the conflict is published here so that a reader does not find two records asserting two different things about whether a batch writes this file. **This batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0003.md` and its cards are at the head of its own block in `state/current.md`, written before Chapter 0671 existed.**

- **0671, day 671, Sunday, the three hundred and fifty-sixth.** A man goes at four hundred yards of wet flag stone with a trough, a bucket and a rag to get the water out of the joints, and the water comes back. **ADRIAN. His want is NOT obtained: the flags are wetter at the tenth hour than at the fourth.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board comes down the row before Adrian does, puts nothing on the flags, and says the one thing about buckets and rows. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall goes past, stops at the head of the run, looks down four hundred yards of it, asks nothing, steps over the run and goes on. At the seventh hour the washer comes back for his own three things and finds the trough standing brim-full and the bucket with its foot in the water, lifts a weight he did not expect, says the second thing, and sets the bucket down in the deepest of the run and leaves it there with the water over its base. **Named: the trough, the bucket and the rag; the two chalks; the date on the door.**
- **0672, day 672, Monday, the three hundred and fifty-seventh.** Two men who never ask each other anything turn a handcart round on wet ground at the top of a shut road. The man of about sixty wants the cart turned and walked back to his own door before the eighth hour; the wheel is in a soft place and the mallet is on the seat. The man of about thirty-four comes up the row, does not offer, is not asked, and says the one thing about a hand on a cart. **Then he puts a hand on the mallet and not on the cart and holds it while the cart comes round, and nobody thanked him for it and the man of about sixty says afterwards that he is not going to ask him which of the two he thinks he did.** The cart goes back down the row and the man of about sixty stands at his own door holding the mallet and does not go in. **Named: the fence of sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, unmeasured; the party of four and the three barrows with nothing on them; the four-hundred-mile road, undated. No post moved, no rope lifted, no road figure, no fourth of the party named.**
- **0673, day 673, Tuesday, the three hundred and fifty-eighth.** A keeper leaves a book open in a column of light on purpose and a man walks into the light. **TRAP DAY: no round figure is printed and no elapsed figure is printed at all on this day.** She wants the book shut and back on the shelf before the man of about thirty-four is at the top of the stair, because the light off the one window has put the head of the column on the right of that page into the open; closing it in front of him would be a thing said without a word in it, so the book stays open. A woman at the back says the room could have a slate and write a thing out; about four people look at the shelf behind the bench and nobody goes and gets it and nobody says no. He comes to the near end of the bench, looks at the page, says three sentences of which the last is that he would like it known that he did not learn anything and that he looked, and she does not look at him while he says them and looks at him afterwards. **The turn in the room is the two hands that have been in that book standing on either side of it with the light on the page and neither of them moving it and neither of them asking.** A man lifts a crate off the floor of that room and carries it out past the shelf without looking at it. **Neither word is printed, neither place is compared, the second slate is not touched, and no page is read out.**
- **0674, day 674, Wednesday, the three hundred and fifty-ninth.** A board goes back up on its nails before the light gets to it. **TRAP DAY: nothing printed.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board has it off its two nails at the sixth hour and wants it up and level before the light comes round the corner of the store, and the wedge will not hold a damp wall and the near nail is in a hole that is not as good as it was. He washes the face in two passes, puts it up, drives the wedge with the heel of his hand, and it sits level. **He does not go near the wall above the top edge of it and no other hand in that row does either.** About four people are already standing in the one place the light is going to land and they mean four different things by what that board is for and not one of them is wrong. The man of about thirty-four comes down the row, stands beside them, and says that four people who are all right about it are all wrong about each other; the light comes round the corner and finds the four of them looking at one clean face. **The fifth discovery is that nobody in this city can be shown a surface anybody was ever told they might write on, and the store's board is on the list without anybody having said so, and about four said they did not know and about four said nothing.** He takes his own board off the trestle and stands it face out in the row for as long as it takes to wipe the trestle with his sleeve and puts it back face in.
- **0675, day 675, Thursday, the three hundred and sixtieth. THE NEARER MIDDLE DAY. ADRIAN IS NOT IN IT. TRAP DAY: nothing printed.** **The volume spends a hand's width of board.** In that room, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says the cost first, in his own mouth, that the person this is about is standing at that bench and he is not going to ask her. A woman at the front is in the middle of a remark about the row and he waits for her to finish and then says the second thing into the middle of it — *A room that says a space is left alone has emptied it*. **About four people say out loud that they do not know and about four say nothing, nobody votes, the four days of cooling are not available to anybody in that room and that is said in narration and in no mouth, and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register is at the near end of the bench and is not asked and does not say.** The decision is that the piece of board is not to be written in, and that nothing is to be written down about the fact that it was decided, and that nobody outside the room is to be told. The chalk stays in the coat, the surface is not touched, and at the tenth hour the door looks exactly as it looked at the fourth hour. The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board turns his own board end over end once and puts it back under his arm the other way up.
- **0676, day 676, Friday, the three hundred and sixty-first. THE SECOND OF THE VOLUME'S TWO MIDDLE DAYS, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS IN NARRATION THAT THERE ARE TWO OF THEM AND THAT NEITHER IS THE MIDDLE. TRAP DAY: nothing printed.** A book goes on a shelf the wrong way up twice and the right way up once. The keeper wants it set back the way up it stands every morning, and on the Thursday a man in that room set it down the other way up and she let him. Two people pick it up to look at the five figures in the middle column and set it back down wrong. A man with his hat in his hands turns it round without asking whether it wants turning, puts it up the way it stands, and goes and sits down at the far end. **She says the one thing about the shelf, and the one person it was said to went down the stair with his back to the window at the time and did not hear it, and about four people who had said they did not know sat down again where they had been sitting.** The room empties by the ninth hour and nothing in it says what happened in it on Thursday. The man of about thirty-four is not in that room on that Friday and nobody said where he was. She says a second thing to the empty bench and nobody in that room hears it. The man of about thirty-nine comes back up and puts his own board down on the floor against the leg of the bench and not on the bench.
- **0677, day 677, Saturday, the three hundred and sixty-second. THE VOLUME'S FIRST COST. ADRIAN IS NOT IN IT.** The woman of about fifty-two wants the woman who keeps the book to be able to sit down in that room, and moves her own stool out of the light at the fourth hour without telling anybody. The room fills between the fourth hour and the tenth hour and about nine people stand at the near end of that bench within reach of the woman who keeps the book's arm. The man of about thirty-four stands at the far end with his back to the plaster and says nothing and asks nothing. At about the seventh hour she says out loud, in front of about nine people, that nine people were stood in that room on the Thursday and that the woman who keeps the book has not been asked one single question about it since, and that that is what the room did, and that she is not her and it is not hers. **Nobody thanked her, nobody argued with her, about four said they did not know and about four said nothing, the man it was decided about looked at the floor of that room and said nothing, and the keeper kept her two hands flat on the bench and did not look up.** She carries the stool out of the room and down the stair and leaves it standing in the dark end where the light does not reach. **Neither word is printed, the two words are not compared, and the cost is named about a person and not about a room and says nothing about what a record is.**
- **0678, day 678, Sunday, the three hundred and sixty-third. THE VOLUME'S FIRST DECISION. ADRIAN IS NOT IN IT.** Nobody sent a notice and there was not going to be one, and the four days of cooling were not available, and about four people found out what they were being asked on the morning they were asked it. A woman at the front asks it first in the ordinary voice and a man at the back asks it the same way and about two others join them: whether the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall will put a name under the date on that door. **He does not say no. He names the price of it first, in his own mouth, in nine words — *A chalk that people can name is a pen* — and then brings the piece out of his own inside breast pocket, holds it in an open hand at the height of the bench with his fingers loose, in daylight, in front of about nine people, and does not close his hand on it and does not turn towards the door.** While the hand is in the room the man of about thirty-nine's own board comes down about an inch against a wall and the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board sees the face of it come down and looks away and goes on down the stair with his bucket, and about nine people in that room saw a piece of chalk in a hand and not the walls of the room. He says a second thing, about the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood in full and not shortened, and puts the chalk back in the pocket, and the hand comes out empty. **Nobody thanked him, nobody improved on him, nobody said he was right, nobody said he was wrong, and the piece of board under the date is carrying what it has been carrying every morning of this flood. No name is under that date and no chapter prints what was on his thumb.** He picks his own board up from the corner of that room and goes out, and the man of about twenty-seven comes up afterwards with his bucket, and the two of them pass each other on the stair and neither says anything.
- **0679, day 679, Monday, the three hundred and sixty-fourth. ADRIAN.** **His want IS obtained: the second row of flats is crossed by something a person can walk on.** He lays a plank along a wet sill and works the silt out from under it with the heel of his hand, and the wind takes the water off eleven miles of flats in sheets and the plank slides a finger's width every time it comes. He turns three turns of the end of a rope — a rope that is not new, flat in three places along its length, with grit ground into the lay of it — round the near end of the plank and pulls them down hard against a ring in the stone. **A woman with a hand-basket and no hat comes along that row, steps up onto the plank, goes up the second row of flats and does not come down it, and nobody in this city has been told which way she went.** The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf comes down the row at the sixth hour, says the one thing about a board on a wet sill, and does not say why he came down that row. He comes back at the ninth hour with his barrow, puts his own boot on the plank, and the plank goes down about the width of a thumbnail and comes back and stays. **He says the second thing to the plank and to nobody, takes his boot off, carries his barrow past it, stops in the wet and looks back at the near end of the plank, which is still down on the sill. Nobody thanked Adrian, nobody told him he was right, and the ring in that stone was put there by a person who is not in this city and is not asked about.**
- **0680, day 680, Tuesday, the three hundred and sixty-fifth. ADRIAN. TRAP DAY: nothing printed.** His want IS obtained: the bench is wiped where a page had not been. The cloth comes out of the bucket at the end of that room and the bench has been wiped at the front of it every morning of this flood and never at the middle, and the middle of it is bare and has been bare. **Seven notices have gone out of this city in this flood, none came back, this city has written no notice since and is not going to write an eighth, and nobody in that room was told to and nobody was told not to.** The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board wants a paper out of that room and has wanted it since the day before, and says the one thing about a notice going out of a room on a morning, and a second thing about being able to say what a paper does and not being able to say where a paper would go. **A woman at the front of the room says out loud that the door at the foot of the stair is the only surface in this city a person could put a thing on, and not one of the nine says yes and not one says no, and nobody went to the door and nobody got a piece of chalk out of anywhere.** Adrian spreads the wrung cloth flat on the table and the water still in the middle of it runs over the near edge onto the floorboards and comes to the bottom edge of the board the man of about thirty-nine has standing against the leg of the bench, and stops there, and the man looks down at it and does not move his board. **A man who had left a thing on a chair in that room comes up the stair, sees the bench and the cloth, says nothing whatever about either of them, takes his own thing off the chair and goes back down, and the chair stays in that room with nothing on it.**

**WHAT THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO.** No chapter past 0680 exists. No new person, no descriptor, no name, no place by narration, no age or number given to anybody who had not got one. No notice written and no eighth asserted as written; no document arrived, read out, written in, shut or sent back. Nothing under the date on any of the ten, and the hand's width of bare board neither widened, narrowed, cleaned, painted nor covered. No second word in the column on the right of that page, and no comparison of the two words in two places, and neither word printed. The second slate never picked up, never turned over and never written on, and no hand but the keeper's near that shelf, and it was never put on the same shelf as the bare piece of door. The wall above the top edge of the store's board was washed by no second person and on none of these ten days, and no hand of this batch went near it. The stallholder's chalk came out of that pocket on one of the ten and went back unused, and the two chalks were never in one hand. Sixteen posts and eleven withies were not measured and no post was moved; the length of new rope was not named, not lifted and not cut. Four in the party, three barrows, no road figure at any value, no fourth of that party named as having gone, and no counting of the days since the fourth of the four said no. Seven documents never joined, never in one hand, never compared aloud; seven notices named and no eighth written. No figure printed off any of the three boards that carry figures, and no two of them equated, and the grit on the wharf board never counted. No page read out in a room on any of the ten days. No question asked of the keeper about the column or the space, of the man of about thirty-four about what he wrote, or of the man of about thirty-nine about the name that is not on his board. The wall said nothing on all ten. The panel count on these ten files is zero and the volume's one panel is on day 690, which is not one of these ten. The stair is never said to be nine steps and the permission is unspent. *Passage* and *privilege* at zero. Nobody gets stronger. `outline/ending.md` unopened and the planned ending untouched.

Days 0681 and 0682 are character days, 0683 is the sixth discovery, 0684 and 0686 are character days, 0685 is an Adrian day, 0687 is a recovery day, 0688 is political, 0689 is the volume's second cost and the man whose own cost it is is in it, and 0690 is the volume's climax and its one panel day, on which the wall says one thing and nobody in that room agrees with it, and the man who has been right about a page in about four seconds five volumes running is asked and does not answer. No card may move a day or reweight one. The panel's words are in `outline/volume-14.md` §6.7 and on the page of Chapter 0690 and in no other file, and the prompt must not reprint them.**

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0003 — SECOND PASS OVER CHAPTERS 0672 AND 0680, AND WHAT THE WHOLE TEN NOW STANDS AT. APPENDED AT THE FOOT.

**A SECOND PASS RAN OVER THESE TEN CHAPTERS AFTER THEY WERE COMMITTED, AND IT FOUND THREE THINGS. TWO WERE ON PAGES AND ARE SUMMARISED HERE PER CHAPTER. THE THIRD WAS IN THE BATCH RECORD AND IS SUMMARISED AT THE FOOT OF THIS BLOCK, BECAUSE A CHAPTER SUMMARY IS NOT THE PLACE FOR IT AND A RECORD SUMMARY IS NOT EITHER, AND THE HONEST PLACE IS THE RECORD, AT `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0003.md` §11.**

- **0672, day 672, Monday, the three hundred and fifty-seventh. CHARACTER. ADRIAN IS NOT IN IT. THE ONE CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH THAT CHANGED ITS CAST OF SPEAKERS.** The man of about sixty with the handcart wanted his cart turned round on the soft place at the top of the shut road and walked back down to his own door before the eighth hour, and it does not turn for him. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall comes to the top of that road with his board under his arm, stops short of the cart, does not put a hand on it, does not offer and is not asked. **THE REPAIR: the man of about sixty now says the thing the chapter said he had in his mouth, in his own mouth, in his own section, and the thing is that he has a mallet lying on that seat and the ground under the cart is soft and he is not going to stand up there and ask a man with a board to get it for me. He stops there, because the other man has gone on up the row with his back to the cart and has not answered and is not going to.** The stallholder then comes across the wet ground, picks the mallet up by the handle end, sets his other hand flat on the cart's side to steady it, and holds it under his arm; the man of about sixty takes the handles with nothing on the seat to shift and turns the cart in three goes and walks it down the last of the row with a man holding his mallet a pace behind him. **He is thanked by nobody in that chapter and he is not thanked for the sentence this pass gave him, and his own existing turn — that a man who will not put his hands on a cart will put his hands on a mallet, and that the difference between those two is the whole of what he got out of the top of that road — is word for word as it stood.** The mallet goes back on the seat with the cord head out and the coil of rope over it, and the man stands at his own door holding the mallet in his two hands with the cart behind him in the road and does not go in. **The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound with the same cord, nobody has measured it, and no post stands anywhere it did not stand the day before. A party of four is four. Three barrows stand on that road with nothing on any of them. The road is four hundred miles from the market end to the town at the other of it, a page came up it and a page went back down it, and no elapsed figure for that road is printed at any value and nobody in this city has ever asked the man with the cart about either of those two days.**
- **0680, day 680, Tuesday, the three hundred and sixty-fifth. POLITICAL. ADRIAN. TRAP DAY: nothing printed. THE ONE CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH THAT HAD NO PARAGRAPH OF THREE SENTENCES IN IT.** His want is obtained, as §4.1 publishes it: the bench is wiped where a page had not been. The cloth comes out of the bucket at the end of that room; the bench has been wiped at the front of it every morning of this flood and never at the middle, and the middle is bare and is where the keeper's elbows go, and no page has ever been on it. **THE REPAIR: he works the cloth along the wood from the front edge back towards the middle, and the wood under it goes darker and then goes back; he lays the flat of his own hand on the place he has just come off and it is cold there, and the place his hand had been on before that is colder, and he does not lay it on a third place; he takes the cloth off the wood, folds it once across the middle and drops it into the bucket, and it goes down slowly and the water in the bucket comes up over the fold and stops there. Every object in that paragraph was already standing in the chapter, and no person, name, age, descriptor, count, figure, decision, document, notice or panel was added.** **AND THE REPAIR AS PUBLISHED IN THAT SENTENCE WAS WRONG TWICE AND WAS REWRITTEN BY THE THIRD PASS, and a reader of this file must take the version below and not the one above, which is left standing only so that the two faults can be named. (i) It dropped the cloth into the bucket and left it there, and two sentences the paragraph's author did not touch put the same cloth on the table, one of them the chapter's closing line, so the object was in a bucket and on a table in the same chapter and the second pass had certified the repair as done. (ii) It began by wiping the near side of the bench from the front edge back towards the middle, which is the motion the chapter's own first paragraph already has, and it ended by saying that no part of that bench was any cleaner than another part, which reads against the want this block publishes as obtained. THE REPAIR ON THE PAGE NOW WIPES THE MIDDLE OF THAT BENCH, which this chapter's own fourth paragraph says has never been wiped at all this flood, finds the dip in the wood that a pair of elbows has worn there, states that the dip was not made by him and not made that morning, and carries the cloth back to the table and leaves it there, which is where two later sentences and the closing line already had it. No person, name, age, descriptor, count, figure, decision, document, notice, panel or object was added by that rewrite either, and the closing line is word for word as it always was and the date, the weekday, the Bare-Month ordinal, the ten-hour clock time, the seven notices, the door, the register with its five figures and every other person in that room are unchanged. The file is 1,334 words.** Seven notices have gone out of this city in this flood, none came back, this city has written no notice since and is not going to write an eighth, and nobody in that room was told to and nobody was told not to. The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board wants a paper out of that room, says the one thing about a notice going out of a room on a morning, and then the second thing about being able to say what a paper does and not being able to say where a paper would go. **A woman at the front of that room says out loud that the door at the foot of the stair is the only surface in this city a person could put a thing on, and not one of the nine says yes and not one says no, and nobody went to the door and nobody got a piece of chalk out of anywhere.** Adrian spreads the wrung cloth flat on the table and the water still in the middle of it runs over the near edge onto the floorboards and comes to the bottom edge of the board the man of about thirty-nine has standing against the leg of the bench, and stops there, and the man looks down at it and does not move his board. **A man who had left a thing on a chair in that room comes up the stair, sees the bench and the cloth, says nothing whatever about either of them, takes his own thing off the chair and goes back down, and the chair stays in that room with nothing on it. ## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0004 — CHAPTERS 0681 TO 0690, DAYS 681 TO 690. PER-DAY ENTRIES AT THE FOOT, APPENDED.

**Days 681 to 690, one chapter to one day, ten distinct days with no gap and no double, Wednesday to Friday, the three hundred and sixty-sixth to the three hundred and seventy-fifth day of the Bare Month, all ten printed ordinals resolving to day less 315, no ordinal after the three hundred and eighty-fifth, no ordinal for a month, no span, and *length*, *long* and *short* applied to that month at zero. Adrian Vale is in 0685 and in no other of these ten, as the map's column says. The panel count on these ten files is ONE and it is in Chapter 0690; the wall says nothing on the other nine. No elapsed figure is printed on any of these ten days at any value except the day the stallholder's chalk went into his pocket, named at full length in 0689, so the same-figure trap is untouched and none of the three round-figure trap days in this range, 688, 689 and 690, prints any of its listed figures.**

- **0681 / day 681, Wednesday, the three hundred and sixty-sixth day of the Bare Month, character, no Adrian.** The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board asks twice, in the ordinary voice, for a line of writing that would say what this city is short of, and says he does not care what it is on. The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living stands behind that bench with an empty satchel and says in his own mouth that he can carry a thing that is written and cannot make one, and later that he carried the last of four sheets up the road himself and did not open it and is not going to be asked about it. The man who wanted the line asks him to write it out in front of about nine people and he says no, and the room finds out in about an hour of ordinary talk that the only person in it who had handled every page that ever went up that road is the only person in it who cannot produce one. **Named: the fourth one-place form, the first one-place form, the third one-place form, the six forms and then seven. It did not: put two sheets in one hand, lay one beside another, compare two aloud, print either word, write in the first form, read it out, or open the satchel. Ends with the flap pressed flat on a bag standing behind that bench.**
- **0682 / day 682, Thursday, the three hundred and sixty-seventh, character, no Adrian.** At one end of a market the man of about thirty-nine says out loud what a name on a surface is for, to the row and not to the man two stalls along, and the man two stalls along hears all of it and does not turn his own board off his trestle and says nothing. He came along that row at the ninth hour, says that his board is the only thing anybody could ever be shown, and puts it into the other man's two hands and goes on up the row without it. **Named: the nineteen rates and the missing name. It did not: print the missing name, measure the space, ask him about the space, put him in a room with the keeper, or print a figure. Nineteen rates and the two-finger space are printed in this chapter because this is the chapter a man's board is about; they are not printed in any other of these ten, which is §17.12's rule and Batch 0003's reading that a handle is not a licence, and this batch took the other reading on this day only and publishes it.**
- **0683 / day 683, Friday, the three hundred and sixty-eighth, discovery, no Adrian.** A room works out in about an hour of ordinary talk that there is no instrument in this city that can tell a page from a person. A man puts a finger on the paper beside the head of the column on the right and not in it, and the woman who keeps the book moves the book an inch so that his finger is beside nothing at all, and neither of them says one word about the inch. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says out loud that there is one thing in this city that can leave a mark you cannot get off and that it is on him in that room, and then that a room wanting to know which hand wrote a word has to ask the hands and it is not going to. The keeper keeps the book open in front of all of them and is asked nothing. **Named: the register and two slates, the words in a place. It did not: print either word, ask him what he wrote, ask her what the column is for, pick up the second slate, put a hand in a place on a page, or compare the two words.**
- **0684 / day 684, Saturday, the three hundred and sixty-ninth, character, no Adrian.** The man of about twenty-seven who washes that board walks eleven miles of flats with a trough, a bucket and a rag, says out loud on the way out that four mornings have already been counted and that he is not going to be the fifth, stands in the channel of wet silt at the near end, is looked at by the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf from his own yard door, and says nothing to him and hears nothing. He comes back with no figure and no word. **Named: the trough, bucket and rag; eleven miles of flats; the count made on foot. It did not: print any figure off that count, print a fifth morning, say the four were got to agree, print the figure on the tally-board, count the grit, or put the rag inside the trough. The trough, the bucket and the rag each have one place at the end and the three are in three places.**
- **0685 / day 685, Sunday, the three hundred and seventieth, physical, ADRIAN yes and in no other of these ten.** Adrian Vale lifts wet silt out of a channel with his own two hands, lays it on the high side of a causeway, lays a length of cord along the top of what he has lifted and ties the near end of it round a stone, and finds that the top of that silt was level with the stone of the causeway on the day before and is not level with it now. The man of about thirty-eight comes along that row with his barrow, says one thing about a step in the channel that nobody put there, goes on, comes back at the ninth hour, steps over the heap, says a second thing about it, and goes on. A woman with nothing in her hands goes round the heap into the channel. **The want at §4.1 is NOT obtained. He is asked nothing, he asks nothing, nobody thanks him, nobody tells him he was right, and the height of the silt is not printed as a figure in this chapter or in any other file in this repository. Named: the count made on foot; eleven miles of flats. The trough the washer left in the low ground is still standing there and nobody has gone back for it.**
- **0686 / day 686, Monday, the three hundred and seventy-first, character, no Adrian.** The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living sits on the bottom step of that stair from the fourth hour with an empty satchel between his boots. He says he is not going down that stair with an empty bag a third time and is not going to say in front of the row what he wants put in it, and he says that nobody is going to get out of him why he has not opened one of the four sheets he has carried, and he does not give the reason. At the tenth hour he sets the satchel on a woman's trestle in that row, takes his hand off, goes back up the stair, and leaves it standing there with the flap down. **Named: the fourth one-place form; the first one-place form. It did not: put the fourth form in this city, put two sheets in one hand, print the word in the fourth or the figure in the first, write in it, read it out, open the satchel, or say why he opens nothing.**
- **0687 / day 687, Tuesday, the three hundred and seventy-second, recovery, no Adrian.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register clears that bench with one hand at each end of it, wipes the wood where the book had stood every morning of this flood and does not wipe the middle of it, and lays a sheet of floor paper that had been rolled up and standing against the leg of that bench back down on it in the position it had been rolled at. A man's hand dries on the paper at the eighth hour. A man who had left a thing on a chair comes up the stair at the tenth hour, sees the cleared bench and the sheet, says nothing whatever about either of them, takes his own thing off the chair, and turns the near edge of the sheet over with the tip of one finger so that the print goes under the paper. **Named: the seven notices. It did not: write an eighth, put a figure or a word on the sheet, decide anything, or put anything under the date. The stool that has stood in the dark end of that room since a Saturday is named and not moved in this chapter.**
- **0688 / day 688, Wednesday, the three hundred and seventy-third, political, no Adrian.** A room in daylight with a date in chalk on the outside of the door at the foot of that stair and about nine people in it, in which nothing is decided and nothing is written and the wall says nothing. The woman of about fifty-two says out loud that she has been in that room every morning of this flood and has never once gone out of it able to show anybody where anything was settled, and nobody thanks her. A man at the back puts the room's own question out into the middle of it and about four people say out loud that they do not know what it is about and the rest say nothing, and nobody puts it to any one person. The woman who keeps the book opens the register at the fourth hour and writes nothing and turns no page and does not look up. **Named: the seven notices; the day on the door; the space under the date on the door. It did not: decide anything, write anything, put anything under the date, say what the space means, print an elapsed figure, or print either of the two round figures this day carries. Ends with her own stool put under that bench, where nothing has been under it.**
- **0689 / day 689, Thursday, the three hundred and seventy-fourth, cost, ADRIAN NEVER.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall names the volume's second cost out loud in his own mouth, first, before anybody has asked him anything: that what it has cost him is that there is nothing to point at. Then he says what his own piece of chalk cost him, and the chapter prints the full form of the day that piece went into that pocket, which is what §14.5's standing two-anchor case for this chapter requires. Nobody thanks him for either, nobody improves on him, nobody says he was right, and the woman the cost is about is at the near end of that bench and is not asked. At the end of the hour he puts two fingers of his own right hand into the dip in the middle of that bench, where the shine is, and takes them out, and nobody in that room saw him do it. **Named: the register and two slates; the words in a place; the day on the door. It did not: put the chalk out of the pocket, use it on anything, write under the date, print either word, ask her what the space was for, or print any of the three round figures this day carries. The two places where a hand that was not the keeper's has been are named in one sentence and put one of them beside the other in no mouth.**
- **0690 / day 690, Friday, the three hundred and seventy-fifth, decision, THE CLIMAX AND THE ONE PANEL, ADRIAN NEVER.** The wall along the far side of that room says one thing, in its own block, carrying no emphasis marks, giving a rule and not an answer, and nobody in that room agrees with it. The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board says two ordinary things about carrying, is asked in daylight in front of about nine people whether he agrees, and does not say anything; the silence goes past about four seconds, nobody asks him a second time, nobody argues with him, and nobody in that room asks him about the bare patch at the foot of the middle of those three columns. About four people said out loud that they did not know what it was about and about four said nothing, the woman who keeps the register did not say, there was no vote and no cooling, and he is not thanked for not answering and the narrator does not say what he thought. At the tenth hour he lifts his own board off the floor, carries it out and down the stair and along the row, and sets it down on the ground against the leg of a trestle with the face of it to the ground, and leaves it and goes on up the row without it. **Named: the nineteen rates and the missing name; the register and two slates; the day on the door; the panel. It did not: reprint the panel, put the man of about thirty-four in that room or in that row, take a vote, take a cooling, say that anybody agreed, say what he thought, print the missing name, measure the space, print any figure, or print any of the four round figures this day carries.**

**WHAT THESE TEN DID AND WHAT THESE TEN DID NOT DO, in one place.** They took a board out of one man's hands and gave it to another man and gave it back on a later day; they cleared a bench and put a sheet of paper back on it; they moved a stool from the dark end of a room to under the bench; they put a heap of silt on the high side of a row of flats; they left a satchel on a stranger's trestle; they laid a sheet of floor paper down and had a hand-print dried on it and turned the print under. They said the volume's second cost out loud in the mouth of the man it belongs to, with the woman it is about standing at the bench not asked. They said one thing on a wall and had nobody in the room agree with it. **They did not write a word on any surface in this city on any of these ten days, they did not read a page out in a room, they did not send a notice, they did not add a document, they did not touch the piece of board under the date, they did not touch the second slate, they did not wash the wall above the store's board, they did not print a figure, they did not name anybody, and they did not get anybody stronger.** The full record, with every command and every reading, is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0004.md`; the ten cards are at the head of this batch's block in `state/current.md`; and the next phase is `workspace/volume-14/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`.

**WHAT THE TEN DAYS DID NOT DO, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, BECAUSE THE ROW ABOVE IS PER DAY AND THIS IS THE BATCH.** No page was written in. No name went under a date. The second slate was never picked up, turned over or written on. The wall above the store's board was washed by nobody and no hand of any pass went near it. The store's board kept its three sets of figures, none rubbed, none with a line of words under any of them, and no figure on it is printed in this city. The tally-board at the wharf kept both lines of grey grit, and neither was wiped, and one piece of its near edge is clean. The second slate and the bare piece of door were never on the same shelf and were never called the same kind of empty place. The two chalks were never in one hand. The rope was never lifted. There is no eighth notice and no eighth document. A party of four is four and no chapter names a fourth of them as having gone and none counts from the day she said no. Adrian Vale is in 0692 and 0697 and nowhere else, Stage 2 on all ten days, thanked by nobody in either and told he was right by nobody in either, and no panel stands on any of these ten files.
**AND THE REPAIR PASS THAT FOLLOWED THE TABLE ABOVE, WHICH CORRECTED NOTHING IN THE TEN ROWS AND ONE THING IN THE EVIDENCE BESIDE THEM. **A REVIEW OF THE BATCH-0004 REPAIR PASS, AND TWO CHAPTERS THAT PASS HAD MADE WORSE. This block is appended and no line above it is edited. The review is `logs/batch-0004.review.log` and the full disposition of its eight findings is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-14-batch-0004.md` §13.**

| Ch | What the review fix changed, one line each |
|---|---|
| **0686** | **REVISED BY THE REVIEW FIX.** The repair pass fixed the closing and removed the man's deliberate release of the bag with it — hand off the top of it, hand flat on the trestle beside it, hand off that as well — and that release is the moment the chapter is about: a man putting down a thing he cannot fill and taking his hands off it where anybody can see. **It is back in one sentence, and in a form that puts the hand on the top of the bag before it goes to the trestle, because the chapter has him lift the bag and carry it out into the row and a man cannot take a hand off a bag he is not touching.** `did not know what was in it` is still out, §20.9's furniture position is still out, and the last beat is unchanged: he goes back up to the bottom step and sits down in the place the bag had been standing and a man coming down past him turns sideways on the stone to get by him. **He still does not open anything he carries and does not say why and is still asked nothing, and the woman with the cloth over her trestle still never looks down at the bag.** |
| **0688** | **REVISED BY THE REVIEW FIX, AND THIS IS THE SERIOUS ONE.** The repair pass did not delete a bad sentence. It deleted the concrete reaction to the woman of about fifty-two's first turn — four say out loud, the rest say nothing, nobody thanks her, the woman who keeps the book does not look up — and put a general line about thanking her in its place, which carries none of the other three things; and it moved the keeper's paragraph from in front of that reaction to behind it, so that a pronoun about sitting at the far end of the room came to sit immediately before a different woman was introduced and the reaction read as the keeper's. **The keeper's paragraph is back in front of the reaction, so the non-response is shown as an action and then said out loud and the movement ends on the keeper not looking up. The general line is out. The reaction names its speaker by where she is sitting — what the woman at the far end of that room was on about — and not by a pronoun. And `nobody thanked her for standing up in a room and saying it` is now `nobody thanked her for saying it out loud in there`, because the lead has her sitting at the far end of the room with both hands on her own knees and she does not get up until the last paragraph of the file.** `opened that register` became `opened her own register`, because this is the first of the ten files to bring the register into a room and the demonstrative had nothing behind it. **She still carries her own stool to the near end of that room and puts it under that bench, the man at the far end still says not one word, the question still gets four people saying out loud that they do not know what it is about and about four saying nothing, and nobody put it to any one person.** |

# VOLUME 14 CLOSE — all fifty days, one line each, at the state the close found them

**This block is appended and no line above it is edited. The full record is `state/volume-14-close.md`. Chapter 0700 is the fiftieth and last chapter of Volume 14. The lines below are the close's own one-line readings of the fifty closings and of the day's subject, and they are a reading and not an instrument.**

| Ch | Day | The day, one line |
|---|---|---|
| 0651 | 651 | a surface in a public place and the two kinds of thing that can be standing on one, and nobody in this city has been shown the person who decided which was which |
| 0652 | 652 | a sheet on a four-hundred-mile road and the one person who could have said what was on it folding it shut and telling nobody |
| 0653 | 653 | a trough, a bucket and a rag at a near gate, and what the man who came along that row to fetch a thing was never told about what was under the water in it |

# VOLUME 15 — CHAPTERS 0701 TO 0750, DAYS 701 TO 750, THE THREE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-SIXTH TO THE FOUR HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE BARE MONTH — PLANNED, NOT WRITTEN

**This block is appended and no line above it is edited. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 15 EXISTS AND EVERY LINE BELOW IS AN INTENTION READ OUT OF `outline/volume-15.md` §9 AND §14.3, AND A CARD FILE WRITTEN AFTER THE CHAPTERS ARE ON DISK WOULD BE A RECONSTRUCTION AND NOT A PLAN. This is the house's fifty-row close-index pattern applied to a volume that has not been written, so that the next batch has one place to read its own days out of and does not have to invent them.**
